This diagram can be identified with an object of
\mathrm{Act}^{2}{Fact}(S)
- the space of composable pairs of active morphisms in
\mathpzc{Fact}
, so it suffices to prove that it is also a Segal space. This follows from the following sequence of isomorphisms

\displaystyle\mathrm{Act}^{2}{Fact}(S)\cong

\displaystyle\underset{(S^{\prime}\twoheadrightarrow S)\in\mathrm{Act}{Fact}(S)}{\mathrm{colim}}\mathrm{Act}{Fact}(S^{\prime})

\displaystyle\cong

\displaystyle\underset{\underset{(S\overset{i}{\rightarrowtail}e)}{\lim}(i_{!}S^{\prime}\twoheadrightarrow e)\in\mathrm{Act}{Fact}(e)}{\mathrm{colim}}\mathrm{Act}{Fact}(S^{\prime})

\displaystyle\cong

\displaystyle\underset{\underset{(S\overset{i}{\rightarrowtail}e)}{\lim}(i_{!}S^{\prime}\twoheadrightarrow e)\in\mathrm{Act}{Fact}(e)}{\mathrm{colim}}\underset{(S\overset{i}{\rightarrowtail}e)}{\lim}\mathrm{Act}{Fact}(i_{!}e)

\displaystyle\cong

\displaystyle\underset{(S\overset{i}{\rightarrowtail}e)}{\lim}\underset{(S^{\prime\prime}\twoheadrightarrow e)\in\mathrm{Act}{Fact}(e)}{\mathrm{colim}}\mathrm{Act}{Fact}(S^{\prime\prime})

\displaystyle\cong

\displaystyle\underset{(S\overset{i}{\rightarrowtail}e)}{\lim}\mathrm{Act}^{2}_{\mathpzc{Fact}}(e),

where the first isomorphism follows from the definition of
\mathrm{Act}^{2}{Fact}(-)
, the second since
\mathrm{Act}{Fact}(-)
satisfies the Segal condition, the third from the relative Segal condition of
2.9
, the fourth from
[
CH19
, Corollary 7.17.]
and the last again from the definition.
∎

Proposition 3.15
.

The constructions of
3.13
endow
\Theta_{2}{Fact}
with a structure of a theory with arities in the sense of
[
Kos21
, Definition 6.2.]
, moreover the category of models for
\Theta{Fact}_{2}
is isomorphic to
2-\mathpzc{Fact}
.

Proof.

To prove the first claim we need to show that the endomorphism
k^{*}k_{!}
of
\mathrm{Hom}_{\mathrm{Cat}}((\Theta_{2}{Fact})^{\mathrm{int}},\mathscr{S})
sends
\mathscr{E}_{\Theta{Fact}_{2}}
to itself. Observe that since active and inert morphisms form a factorization system on
\Theta_{2}{Fact}
we have

k^{*}k_{!}\mathscr{F}(S;\overline{n};\overline{m})\cong\underset{((S^{\prime};\overline{n}^{\prime};\overline{m}^{\prime})\rightarrow(S;\overline{n};\overline{m}))\in(k/(S;\overline{n};\overline{m}))}{\mathrm{colim}}\mathscr{F}(S^{\prime};\overline{n}^{\prime};\overline{m}^{\prime})\cong\underset{((S^{\prime};\overline{n}^{\prime};\overline{m}^{\prime})\twoheadrightarrow(S;\overline{n};\overline{m}))\in\mathrm{Act}_{\Theta_{2}{Fact}}(S;\overline{n};\overline{m})}{\mathrm{colim}}\mathscr{F}(S^{\prime};\overline{n}^{\prime};\overline{m}^{\prime}).

Assume
S=e_{1}e_{2}...e_{n+m}
, then it is easy to see that

(9)

\displaystyle(\Theta{Fact}_{2})^{\mathrm{el}}_{(S;l_{1},...,l_{n+m})/}\cong\mathpzc{Cat}^{\mathrm{el}}_{[l_{1}]/}\coprod_{*}\mathpzc{Cat}^{\mathrm{el}}_{[l_{1}]/}\coprod_{*}...\coprod_{*}\mathpzc{Cat}^{\mathrm{el}}_{[l_{n+m}]/}.

Observe that by the definition of an active morphism
\mathrm{Act}_{\Theta{Fact}_{2}}(e,l)
for
e\in\{h,v\}
is isomorphic to the space of pairs
(a:[1]\twoheadrightarrow[s],\{b_{i}:[l]\twoheadrightarrow[t_{i}]\}|_{i\in\{1,2,...,s\}})
. Now observe that, since both
\mathrm{Act}{Fact}(S)
and
\mathrm{Act}{Cat}([n])
satisfy the respective Segal conditions, we have

\mathrm{Act}_{\Theta_{2}{Fact}}(S;\overline{l})\cong\mathrm{Act}_{\Theta_{2}{Fact}}(e_{1},1)^{\times l_{1}}\times\mathrm{Act}_{\Theta_{2}{Fact}}(e_{2},1)^{\times l_{2}}\times...\times\mathrm{Act}_{\Theta_{2}{Fact}}(e_{n+m},1)^{\times l_{n+m}}.

It follows from
eq.

9
that this is indeed a
\Theta_{2}{Fact}
Segal space. Now assume that

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(S^{\prime};\overline{l}^{\prime})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(S;\overline{l})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces i_{!}(S^{\prime};\overline{l}^{\prime})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(e;1)}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces a}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces a^{*}i}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces i}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces i_{!}a}

is a factorization square in
\Theta_{2}{Fact}
in which
i
corresponds to the inclusion of an elementary 2-cell in
S
. Assume that the restriction of
a
to
e
is given by a pair
([1]\twoheadrightarrow[s];\{[1]\twoheadrightarrow[t_{i}]\}|_{i\in\{1,2,...,s\}})
and that
a\circ i(0)=j
, then
i_{!}(S^{\prime};\overline{l}^{\prime})
is a subcategory of
(S^{\prime};\overline{l}^{\prime})
containing all objects between
j
and
j+n
and all 2-cells between
j+i
and
j+i+1
that lie in the subinterval
[t_{i}]\subset[l_{j+i}]
. Those subcategories cover
(S^{\prime};\overline{l}^{\prime})
and only intersect along 0-cells and 1-cells. It follows from
eq.

9
that the natural morphism

\underset{((S;\overline{l})\overset{i}{\rightarrowtail}e)\in(\Theta{Fact}_{2})^{\mathrm{el}}_{(S;\overline{l})/}}{\lim}\underset{(i_{!}(S^{\prime};\overline{l}^{\prime})\rightarrowtail e_{0})\in(\Theta{Fact}_{2})^{\mathrm{el}}_{i_{!}(S^{\prime};\overline{l}^{\prime})/}}{\lim}\mathscr{F}(e_{0})\rightarrow\underset{((S^{\prime};\overline{l}^{\prime})\rightarrowtail e^{\prime})\in(\Theta{Fact}_{2})^{\mathrm{el}}_{(S^{\prime};\overline{l}^{\prime})/}}{\lim}\mathscr{F}(e)

is an isomorphism for all functor
\mathscr{F}:\Theta_{2}{Fact}\rightarrow\mathscr{S}
. With this we can conclude the proof of the first claim just like in
2.9
.

To prove the claim about the category of models, we first observe that there is a natural functor
q:2-\mathpzc{Fact}\rightarrow\Theta{Fact}_{2}
that sends
(S,n)
to

(S;\overbrace{n,n,...,n}^{\text{n+m times}}).

We will prove that
q_{!}
restricts to an isomorphism
\mathrm{Fact}\xrightarrow{\sim}\mathrm{Mod}_{\Theta{Fact}_{2}}(\mathscr{S})
. We first prove that for every
\mathscr{F}\in\mathrm{Mod}_{\Theta{Fact}_{2}}(\mathscr{S})
we have
q_{!}q^{*}\mathscr{F}\cong\mathscr{F}
. By definition we have

q_{!}q^{*}\mathscr{F}(S;\overline{l})\cong\underset{(q(S^{\prime},n^{\prime})\rightarrow(S;\overline{l}))\in(q/(S;\overline{l}))}{\mathrm{colim}}\mathscr{F}(S^{\prime},n^{\prime}).

Denote by
(q/(S;\overline{l}))^{\mathrm{e.p.}}
the full subcategory of
(q/(S;\overline{l}))
on those morphisms
(S;\overline{l})\xrightarrow{f}q(S,n)
in
(\Theta_{2}{Fact})^{\mathrm{op}}
for which the underlying morphism in
\mathpzc{Fact}^{\mathrm{op}}
is active. Observe that every morphism
(S;\overline{l})\xrightarrow{s}q(S^{\prime},n^{\prime})
in
(\Theta_{2}{Fact})^{\mathrm{op}}
uniquely decomposes as

(S;\overline{l})\xrightarrow{f}q(S^{\prime\prime},n^{\prime\prime})\xrightarrow{q(i,\mathrm{id})}q(S^{\prime},n^{\prime}),

where
f\in(q/(S;\overline{l}))^{\mathrm{e.p.}}
and
(i,\mathrm{id})
is morphism in
(2-\mathpzc{Fact})^{\mathrm{op}}\cong\mathpzc{Fact}^{\mathrm{op}}\times\Delta
for which
i
is an inert morphism in
\mathpzc{Fact}^{\mathrm{op}}
. It follows that
(q/(S;\overline{l}))^{\mathrm{e.p.}}\hookrightarrow(q/(S;\overline{l}))
is cofinal. Also observe that it follows from
lemma

3.14
that

(q/(S;\overline{l}))^{\mathrm{e.p.}}\cong(q/(e_{1};l_{1}))^{\mathrm{e.p.}}\times(q/(e_{2};l_{2}))^{\mathrm{e.p.}}\times...\times(q/(e_{n+m};l_{n+m}))^{\mathrm{e.p.}}.

Every active morphism
a:S\twoheadrightarrow S^{\prime\prime}
in
\mathpzc{Fact}^{\mathrm{op}}
defines a decomposition of
S^{\prime\prime}
into substrings
S^{\prime\prime}_{i}
for
i\in\{1,2,...,l(S)\}
where each
S^{\prime\prime}_{i}
is given by the image of the
i
th elementary segment of S and
l(S)
denotes the length of the string
S
. Moreover, it follows from the Segal condition that

\mathscr{F}(q(S^{\prime\prime},n^{\prime\prime}))\cong\mathscr{F}(q(S_{1},n^{\prime\prime}))\times_{\mathscr{F}(*)}\mathscr{F}(q(S_{2},n^{\prime\prime}))\times_{\mathscr{F}(*)}...\times_{\mathscr{F}(*)}\mathscr{F}(q(S_{n+m},n^{\prime\prime})).

Putting everything together we get the following string of isomorphisms

\displaystyle q_{!}q^{*}\mathscr{F}(S;\overline{l})\cong

\displaystyle\underset{(q(S^{\prime},n^{\prime})\rightarrow(S;\overline{l}))\in(q/(S;\overline{l}))}{\mathrm{colim}}\mathscr{F}(S^{\prime},n^{\prime})

\displaystyle\cong

\displaystyle\underset{(q(S^{\prime\prime},n^{\prime\prime})\rightarrow(S;\overline{l}))\in(q/(S;\overline{l}))^{\mathrm{e.p.}}}{\mathrm{colim}}\mathscr{F}(S^{\prime\prime},n^{\prime\prime})

\displaystyle\cong

\displaystyle\underset{\bigtimes_{i=1}^{l(S)}(q/(e_{i};l_{i}))^{\mathrm{e.p.}}}{\mathrm{colim}}\mathscr{F}(q(S_{1},n^{\prime\prime}))\times_{\mathscr{F}(*)}\mathscr{F}(q(S_{2},n^{\prime\prime}))\times_{\mathscr{F}(*)}...\times_{\mathscr{F}(*)}\mathscr{F}(q(S_{n+m},n^{\prime\prime}))

\displaystyle\cong

\displaystyle\underset{(q/(e_{1};l_{1}))^{\mathrm{e.p.}}}{\mathrm{colim}}\mathscr{F}(q(S_{1},n^{\prime\prime}))\times_{\mathscr{F}(*)}...\times_{\mathscr{F}(*)}\underset{(q/(e_{l(S)};l_{l(S)}))^{\mathrm{e.p.}}}{\mathrm{colim}}\mathscr{F}(q(S_{l(S)},n^{\prime\prime}))

\displaystyle\cong

\displaystyle q_{!}q^{*}\mathscr{F}(e_{1};l_{1})\times_{q_{!}q^{*}\mathscr{F}(*)}q_{!}q^{*}\mathscr{F}(e_{2};l_{2})\times_{q_{!}q^{*}\mathscr{F}(*)}...\times_{q_{!}q^{*}\mathscr{F}(*)}q_{!}q^{*}\mathscr{F}(e_{l(S)};l_{l(S)}).

It follows that it suffices to prove the isomorphism for strings
(e;l)
of length one. However, observe that in this case
(q/(e;l))^{\mathrm{e.p.}}
has a final object given by the identity morphism and the claim follows. Finally, observe that the calculations above also imply that
p^{*}p_{!}\mathscr{F}\cong\mathscr{F}
for
\mathscr{F}\in 2-\mathrm{Fact}
thus concluding the proof.
∎

Lemma 3.16
.

The functor
q:2-\mathpzc{Fact}\rightarrow\Theta{Fact}_{2}
constructed in
3.15
is cofinal.

Proof.

We need to prove that for every object
(S;\overline{n};\overline{m})
of
\Theta_{2}{Fact}
the category
((S;\overline{n};\overline{m})/q)
is contractible. An object of this category can be identified with a morphism
q(S^{\prime},r)\xrightarrow{f}(S;\overline{n};\overline{m})
in
(\Theta_{2}{Fact})^{\mathrm{op}}
. Denote by
((S;\overline{n};\overline{m})/q)^{\mathrm{f.i.}}
the full subcategory of
((S;\overline{n};\overline{m})/q)
on those morphisms
q(S^{\prime},r)\xrightarrow{i}(S;\overline{n};\overline{m})
for which the underlying morphism
S^{\prime}\rightarrow S
in
\mathpzc{Fact}^{\mathrm{op}}
is inert. Observe that we can uniquely factor any morphism
q(S^{\prime},r)\xrightarrow{f}(S;\overline{n};\overline{m})
as

q(S,r)\xrightarrow{(q(a),\mathrm{id})}q(S^{\prime},r^{\prime})\xrightarrow{i}(S;\overline{n};\overline{m}),

where
a
is an active morphism in
\mathpzc{Fact}^{\mathrm{op}}
and
i
belongs to
((S;\overline{n};\overline{m})/q)^{\mathrm{f.i.}}
. It follows that
((S;\overline{n};\overline{m})/q)^{\mathrm{f.i.}}\hookrightarrow((S;\overline{n};\overline{m})/q)
is cofinal, in particular it induces an equivalence on classifying spaces.

Observe that there is a natural forgetful functor
U:((S;\overline{n};\overline{m})/q)^{\mathrm{f.i.}}\rightarrow\mathpzc{Fact}^{\mathrm{int}}_{S/}
which is moreover a coCartesian fibration. Since
\mathpzc{Fact}^{\mathrm{int}}_{S/}
is obviously contractible, to prove the claim it suffices to demonstrate that the fiber of
U
over a given
S^{\prime}\overset{i}{\rightarrowtail}S
in
\mathpzc{Fact}^{\mathrm{op}}
is contractible. Assume that
S^{\prime}
has
n^{\prime}
horizontal segments and
m^{\prime}
vertical segments and that
i_{h}(0)=j_{0}
,
i_{h}(n^{\prime})=j_{1}
,
i_{v}(0)=k_{0}
and
i_{v}(m^{\prime})=k_{1}
. Observe that an object of
U^{-1}(i)
can be identified with an object
[l]\in\Delta
together with morphisms
f_{i}:[l]\rightarrow[n_{i}]
for
i\in\{j_{0},j_{0}+1,...,j_{1}\}
and
g_{s}:[n]\rightarrow[m_{s}]
for
s\in\{k_{0},k_{0}+1,...,k_{1}\}
. A morphism in
U^{-1}(i)
is given by a morphism
[l]\rightarrow[l^{\prime}]
making an obvious diagram commute. In other words,
U^{-1}(i)
is isomorphic to
((\Delta^{\mathrm{op}})^{k_{1}-k_{0}+j_{1}-j_{0}}/\Delta)
, where
\Delta:\Delta^{\mathrm{op}}\rightarrow(\Delta^{\mathrm{op}})^{k_{1}-k_{0}+j_{1}-j_{0}}
is the diagonal morphism. Finally, observe that
((\Delta^{\mathrm{op}})^{k_{1}-k_{0}+j_{1}-j_{0}}/\Delta)
is contractible since
\Delta^{\mathrm{op}}
is sifted
[
Lur09
, Lemma 5.5.8.4.]
.
∎

Construction 3.17
.

Given pair of objects
\theta(n;a_{1},...,a_{n})
and
\theta(m;b_{1},...,b_{m})
we can consider a bicategory
\theta(n;a_{1},..,a_{n})\otimes\theta(m;b_{1},...,b_{m})
, where
\otimes
denotes the Gray product of
3.7
. More explicitly, the objects of
\theta(n;a_{1},..,a_{n})\otimes\theta(m;b_{1},...,b_{m})
are given by pairs
(i,j)
with
0\leq i\leq n
and
0\leq j\leq m
, a morphism from
(i,j)
to
(i^{\prime},j^{\prime})
is given by the data of a morphism
f:i\rightarrow i^{\prime}
in
\theta(n;\overline{a})
, a morphism
s:j\rightarrow j^{\prime}
in
\theta(m;\overline{b})
and
i^{\prime}-i
marked points
j\leq j_{1}\leq...\leq j_{i^{\prime}-i}\leq j^{\prime}
. Now, given an object
(S;h_{1},...,h_{n};v_{1},...,v_{m})
of
\Theta{Fact}_{2}
we can associate to it a bicategory
\mathrm{Sq}^{\mathrm{lax}}(S;\overline{h};\overline{v})
given by a full subcategory of
\theta(n;h_{1},..,h_{n})\otimes\theta(m;v_{1},...,v_{m})
on objects lying above
S
. This defines a functor
\Theta_{2}{Fact}\rightarrow 2-\mathrm{Cat}
which we can view as a correspondence
\widehat{\mathscr{L}}:\Theta_{2}{Fact}\nrightarrow\Delta^{\mathrm{op}}\times\Delta^{\mathrm{op}}
.

Corollary 3.18
.

Given an object
\mathscr{F}\in\mathpzc{Fact}
,
3.15
allows us to view it both as a functor
\mathscr{F}_{0}:2-\mathpzc{Fact}\rightarrow\mathscr{S}
and as a functor
\mathscr{F}_{1}:\Theta_{2}{Fact}\rightarrow\mathscr{S}
. Then there is an isomorphism

\widetilde{\mathscr{L}}\mathscr{F}_{0}\cong\widehat{\mathscr{L}}\mathscr{F}_{1}.

Proof.

By definition we have

\widehat{\mathscr{L}}\mathscr{F}_{1}(s,t)\cong\underset{\mathrm{Sq}^{\mathrm{lax}}(S;\overline{h};\overline{v})\in\Theta{Fact}_{2})}{\mathrm{colim}}\mathrm{Hom}_{2-\mathrm{Cat}}(\theta(s;\overline{a}),\mathrm{Sq}^{\mathrm{lax}}(S;\overline{h};\overline{v}))\times\mathscr{F}_{1}(S;\overline{h};\overline{v}).

Recall from
lemma

3.16
that the natural functor
q:2-\mathpzc{Fact}\rightarrow\Theta_{2}{Fact}
is cofinal, so we can replace the colimit over
\Theta{Fact}_{2}
by a colimit over
2-\mathpzc{Fact}
. The claim now follows, since by definition
\mathscr{F}_{0}
and
\mathscr{F}_{1}
agree on
2-\mathpzc{Fact}
.
∎

Construction 3.19
.

Given objects
\mathscr{F}\in\mathrm{Fact}
and
\mathscr{M}\in 2-\mathrm{Fact}
, we can consider both as coCartesian fibrations over
\mathpzc{Fact}
that satisfy the Segal conditions. We will call a
categorical lax functor
from
\mathscr{F}
to
\mathscr{M}
a morphism of categories over
\mathpzc{Fact}
that takes coCartesian morphisms over inert morphisms in
\mathpzc{Fact}
to coCartesian morphisms. We will denote by
\mathrm{Hom}^{\mathrm{lax}}_{\mathrm{Cat}}(\mathscr{F},\mathscr{M})
the space of categorical lax functors.

Proposition 3.20
.

There is a bicategory with a factorization system
L^{\mathrm{lax}}\mathscr{F}
such that

\mathrm{Hom}_{2-\mathrm{Fact}}(L^{\mathrm{lax}}\mathscr{F},\mathscr{M})\cong\mathrm{Hom}^{\mathrm{lax}}_{\mathrm{Cat}}(\mathscr{F},\mathscr{M}).

Moreover, we have an isomorphism

L^{\mathrm{lax}}\mathscr{F}\cong\mathscr{F}\times_{\mathpzc{Fact}}\mathrm{Ar}^{\mathrm{act}}(\mathpzc{Fact}),

where
\mathrm{Ar}^{\mathrm{act}}(\mathpzc{Fact})
denotes the coCartesian fibration over
\mathpzc{Fact}
sending
S
to
\mathpzc{Fact}^{\mathrm{act}}_{/S}
.

Proof.

A lax functor can be identified with a morphism
\mathscr{F}\rightarrow\mathscr{M}
in
\mathrm{coCart}^{\mathpzc{Fact}^{\mathrm{int}}}(\mathpzc{Fact})
in the notation of
[
Kos21
, Construction 4.10.]
, the fact that the required left adjoint exists now follows from
[
Kos21
, Proposition 4.11.]
. Since active and inert morphisms form a factorization system on
\mathpzc{Fact}
, the same argument as in
[
Kos21
, Proposition 4.23.]
shows that the formula for
L^{\mathrm{lax}}\mathscr{F}
is correct. To finish the proof it only remains to demonstrate that
\mathscr{F}\times_{\mathpzc{Fact}}\mathrm{Ar}^{\mathrm{act}}(\mathpzc{Fact})
satisfies the Segal condition, however this follows from
lemma

3.14
.
∎

Notation 3.21
.

Given an object
\mathscr{F}\in\mathrm{Fact}
we will denote by
\mathscr{L}^{\mathrm{lax}}\mathscr{F}
the composition
\mathscr{L}L^{\mathrm{lax}}\mathscr{F}
of functors of
3.19
and
3.5
. For an arbitrary twofold Segal space
\mathscr{B}
we will introduce the following notation

\mathrm{Hom}^{\mathrm{lax}}_{\mathrm{Fact}}(\mathscr{F},\mathscr{B})\overset{\mathrm{def}}{=}\mathrm{Hom}^{\mathrm{lax}}_{2-\mathrm{Cat}}(\mathscr{L}^{\mathrm{lax}}\mathscr{F},\mathscr{B})

(where
\mathrm{Hom}^{\mathrm{lax}}_{2-\mathrm{Cat}}(\mathscr{L}^{\mathrm{lax}}\mathscr{F},\mathscr{B})
is the twofold Segal space of functors and lax natural transformations of
[
Kos21
, Definition 4.6.]
) and call
\mathrm{Hom}^{\mathrm{lax}}_{\mathrm{Fact}}(\mathscr{F},\mathscr{B})
the
bicategory of lax functors
. We refer to objects of
\mathrm{Hom}^{\mathrm{lax}}_{2-\mathrm{Cat}}(\mathscr{L}^{\mathrm{lax}}\mathscr{F},\mathscr{B})
as
lax functors
and denote them by
F:\mathscr{F}\rightsquigarrow\mathscr{B}
.

Proposition 3.22
.

For any
\mathscr{F}\in\mathrm{Fact}
and
\mathscr{B}\in 2-\mathrm{Cat}
we have

\mathrm{Hom}^{\mathrm{lax}}_{\mathrm{Fact}}(\mathscr{F},\mathscr{B})\cong\underset{(\mathrm{Sq}(S)\rightarrow\mathscr{F})\in\mathpzc{Fact}/\mathscr{F}}{\lim}\mathrm{Hom}^{\mathrm{lax}}_{\mathrm{Fact}}(\mathrm{Sq}(S),\mathscr{B}).

Proof.

This follows from
lemma

3.6
and he same argument as in
[
Kos21
, Proposition 4.34]
.
∎

Construction 3.23
.

Fix an integer
n\geq 0
, we will construct of a certain discrete
n
-category
\mathrm{Or}(n)
called the
nth oriental
. This construction was originally presented in
[
Str87
]
, however numerous other expositions exist, including
[
Ste06
]
,
[
Str91
]
,
[
KV91
]
,
[
Ver08
]
. The
k
-morphisms of
\mathrm{Or}(n)
can be identified with certain sets of faces of the
n
-simplex of dimension
\leq k
(by a face of dimension
l
we mean an injective morphism
[l]\rightarrow[n]
), so we first introduce some notation: given a
k
-face
u:[k]\rightarrow[n]
we denote by
\partial_{+}u
(resp.
\partial_{-}u
) the union of even (resp. odd)
(k-1)
-faces of
u
, in other words

\partial_{+}u\overset{\mathrm{def}}{=}\bigcup_{0\leq i\leq n,i\;\mathrm{even}}u\circ\delta_{i},\;\;\;\;\;\partial_{-}u\overset{\mathrm{def}}{=}\bigcup_{0\leq i\leq n,i\;\mathrm{odd}}u\circ\delta_{i}.

For a set
S\overset{\mathrm{def}}{=}\{u_{j}\}_{j\in J}
of
k
-faces we define
\partial_{\pm}
to be the union of
\partial_{\pm}u_{j}
over
J
.

Now we define the set of
k
-morphisms to be the set of sequences

x\overset{\mathrm{def}}{=}(x_{0}^{+},x_{0}^{-}|x_{1}^{-},x_{1}^{+}|...|x_{k}^{+},x_{k}^{-})

in which
x_{i}^{\pm}
are (possibly empty) sets of
i
-faces of
[n]
such that

•

x_{0}^{\pm}
are singletons;

•

No object
u\in x_{i}^{\pm}
belongs to
\partial_{+}u_{1}
and
\partial_{+}u_{2}
(resp.
\partial_{-}u_{1}
and
\partial_{-}u_{2}
) for a pair of distinct objects
u_{1,2}
of
x_{i+1}^{+}
or
x_{i+1}^{-}
;

•

\partial_{+}x_{i+1}^{+}\bigcup\partial_{-}x_{i+1}^{+}\backslash(\partial_{+}x_{i+1}^{+}\bigcap\partial_{-}x_{i+1}^{+})\cong\partial_{+}x_{i+1}^{-}\bigcup\partial_{-}x_{i+1}^{-}\backslash(\partial_{+}x_{i+1}^{-}\bigcap\partial_{-}x_{i+1}^{-})\cong x_{i}^{+}\bigcup x_{i}^{-}
.

For a positive number
p\leq k
we define the
p
th source
d_{p}^{-}x
(resp.
p
th target
d_{p}^{+}x
) to be given by

(x_{0}^{+},x_{0}^{-}|x_{1}^{-},x_{1}^{+}|...|x_{p-1}^{+},x_{p-1}^{-}|x_{p}^{-},x_{p}^{-})

(resp. by

(x_{0}^{+},x_{0}^{-}|x_{1}^{-},x_{1}^{+}|...|x_{p-1}^{+},x_{p-1}^{-}|x_{p}^{+},x_{p}^{+})).

If
d^{+}_{p}x\cong d^{-}_{p}y\cong w
, then we define
y*_{p}x
to be given by

(w_{0}^{-},w_{1}^{+}|...|w_{p-1}^{-},w_{p-1}^{+}|x_{p}^{-}\cup y_{p}^{-},x_{p}^{+}\cup y_{p}^{+}|...|x_{k}^{-}\cup y_{k}^{-},x_{k}^{+}\cup y_{k}^{+}).

Now, assume that we are given an
n
-truncated simplicial space
X_{\bullet}:\Delta^{\mathrm{op}}_{\leq n}\rightarrow\mathscr{S}
, we will associate to it an
n
-category
n-\mathrm{Cat}(X_{\bullet})
. To do this, first observe that to any
k
-morphism
x
of
\mathrm{Or}(n)
we can associate a simplicial set
s(x)
given by the union of all faces contained in
x
. Similarly, for any composable sequence of morphisms
a\in\mathrm{Or}(n)_{\overline{m}}
for
[\overline{m}]\overset{\mathrm{def}}{=}([m_{1}],[m_{2}],...,[m_{n}])\in(\Delta^{\mathrm{op}})^{n}
(here we view
\mathrm{Or}(n)
as a functor
(\Delta^{\mathrm{op}})^{n}\rightarrow\mathscr{S}
) we define
s(a)
to be the simplicial set associated to their composition. Given a
p
-composable pair of
k
-morphism
x_{1}
and
x_{2}
observe that

s(x_{1}*_{p}x_{2})\cong s(x_{1})\coprod_{s(d+_{p}(x_{1}))}s(x_{2}).

Iterating this observation we get that for
a\in\mathrm{Or}(n)_{\overline{m}}
we have

s(a)\cong\underset{(i:[\overline{m}]\rightarrowtail e)\in(\Delta^{\mathrm{op},n})^{\mathrm{el}}_{[\overline{m}]/}}{\mathrm{colim}}s(i_{!}[\overline{m}]).

Now consider a functor
F:(\Delta^{\mathrm{op}})^{n}\rightarrow\mathrm{Cat}
sending
[\overline{m}]
to the category whose objects are given by
a\in\mathrm{Or}(n)_{\overline{m}}
and whose morphisms are given by
surjective
morphisms between
s(a)
. Observe that, if for a
p
-composable pair of
k
-morphisms
x_{1,2}
we are given surjective morphisms
f:s(x_{1})\rightarrow s(y_{1})
and
g:s(x_{2})\rightarrow s(y_{2})
that agree on
d^{+}_{p}x_{1}
, we can define a surjective morphism

f\sqcup g:s(x_{1})\coprod_{s(d+_{p}(x_{1}))}s(x_{2})\rightarrow s(y_{1})\coprod_{s(d+_{p}(y_{1}))}s(y_{2}).

It follows that

F([1],...,\overset{p}{[2]},...,\overset{k}{[1]},...,[0])\cong[F([1],...,\overset{p}{[1]},...,\overset{k}{[1]}...,[0])\times_{[F([1],...,\overset{p}{[1]},...,[0])}[F([1],...,\overset{p}{[1]},...,\overset{k}{[1]},...,[0]).

By iterating this statement we get

F([\overline{m}])\cong\underset{([\overline{m}]\rightarrowtail e)\in(\Delta^{\mathrm{op},n})^{\mathrm{el}}_{[\overline{m}]/}}{\lim}F(e).

Finally, we define the functor
n-\mathrm{Cat}(X_{\bullet}):(\Delta^{\mathrm{op}})^{n}\rightarrow\mathscr{S}
by

n-\mathrm{Cat}(X_{\bullet})([\overline{m}])\overset{\mathrm{def}}{=}\underset{a\in F([\overline{m}])^{\mathrm{op}}}{\mathrm{colim}}\mathrm{Hom}_{\mathscr{P}(\Delta_{\leq n})}(s(a),X_{\bullet}).

Combining the observations made above we see that

\displaystyle n-\mathrm{Cat}(X_{\bullet})([\overline{m}])\overset{\mathrm{def}}{=}

\displaystyle\underset{a\in F([\overline{m}])^{\mathrm{op}}}{\mathrm{colim}}\mathrm{Hom}_{\mathscr{P}(\Delta_{\leq n})}(s(a),X_{\bullet})

\displaystyle\cong

\displaystyle\underset{a\in\underset{([\overline{m}]\rightarrowtail e)\in(\Delta^{\mathrm{op},n})^{\mathrm{el}}_{[\overline{m}]/}}{\lim}F(e)^{\mathrm{op}}}{\mathrm{colim}}\mathrm{Hom}_{\mathscr{P}(\Delta_{\leq n})}(\underset{(i:[\overline{m}]\rightarrowtail e)\in(\Delta^{\mathrm{op},n})^{\mathrm{el}}_{[\overline{m}]/}}{\mathrm{colim}}s(i_{!}[\overline{m}]),X_{\bullet})

\displaystyle\cong

\displaystyle\underset{a\in\underset{([\overline{m}]\rightarrowtail e)\in(\Delta^{\mathrm{op},n})^{\mathrm{el}}_{[\overline{m}]/}}{\lim}F(e)^{\mathrm{op}}}{\mathrm{colim}}\underset{(i:[\overline{m}]\rightarrowtail e)\in(\Delta^{\mathrm{op},n})^{\mathrm{el}}_{[\overline{m}]/}}{\lim}\mathrm{Hom}_{\mathscr{P}(\Delta_{\leq n})}(s(i_{!}[\overline{m}]),X_{\bullet})

\displaystyle\cong

\displaystyle\underset{(i:[\overline{m}]\rightarrowtail e)\in(\Delta^{\mathrm{op},n})^{\mathrm{el}}_{[\overline{m}]/}}{\lim}\underset{a\in F(e)^{\mathrm{op}}}{\mathrm{colim}}\mathrm{Hom}_{\mathscr{P}(\Delta_{\leq n})}(s(i_{!}a),X_{\bullet})

\displaystyle\cong

\displaystyle\underset{(i:[\overline{m}]\rightarrowtail e)\in(\Delta^{\mathrm{op},n})^{\mathrm{el}}_{[\overline{m}]/}}{\lim}n-\mathrm{Cat}(X_{\bullet})(e).

It follows that
n-\mathrm{Cat}(X_{\bullet})
is indeed an
n
-category.

Observe that for every morphism
X_{\bullet}\rightarrow Y_{\bullet}
of
n
-truncated simplicial spaces there is an induced morphism
n-\mathrm{Cat}(X_{\bullet})\rightarrow n-\mathrm{Cat}(Y\bullet)
. In particular, for a simplicial object
X_{\bullet}:\Delta^{\mathrm{op}}\rightarrow\mathscr{S}
we have morphisms
n-\mathrm{Cat}(X_{\leq s})\rightarrow n-\mathrm{Cat}(X_{\leq s+1})
for all
s\geq 0
, and we define

\infty-\mathrm{Cat}(X_{\bullet})\overset{\mathrm{def}}{=}\mathrm{colim}(0-\mathrm{Cat}(X_{\leq 0})\rightarrow 1-\mathrm{Cat}(X_{\leq 1})\rightarrow...s-\mathrm{Cat}(X_{\leq s})\rightarrow...),

where the colimit is taken in the
\infty-\mathrm{Cat}
.

Remark 3.24
.

The
\infty
-category
\infty-\mathrm{Cat}(X_{\bullet})
can essentially be described as a free
\infty
-category on a simplicial space: its objects are the elements of
X_{0}
, its (non-unital) morphisms are given by composable sequences of nondegenerate elements in
X_{1}
, for every nondegenerate
x_{2}\in X_{2}
there is a 2-morphism
\delta_{2}x_{2}\circ\delta_{0}x_{2}\rightarrow\delta_{1}x_{2}
etc. In general,
k
-morphisms in
\infty-\mathrm{Cat}(X_{\bullet})
are generated under composition by
k
-simplices of
X_{\bullet}
, with degenerate
k
-simplices corresponding to identity morphisms.

Notation 3.25
.

Denote by
p_{1}:(\Delta^{\mathrm{op}})^{\infty}\rightarrow\Delta^{\mathrm{op}}
the projection to the first coordinate. For a simplicial space
X_{\bullet}
we denote

\widetilde{L_{\mathrm{Cat}}}(X_{\bullet})\overset{\mathrm{def}}{=}p_{1,!}(\infty-\mathrm{Cat}(X_{\bullet})).

In particular, we see that

\widetilde{L_{\mathrm{Cat}}}(X_{\bullet})_{0}\cong\underset{(\Delta^{\mathrm{op}})^{\infty}}{\mathrm{colim}}X_{0}\cong X_{0}.

More generally, observe that we have the following sequence of isomorphisms

\displaystyle\widetilde{L_{\mathrm{Cat}}}(X_{\bullet})_{n}\cong

\displaystyle\underset{[\overline{m}]\in\Delta^{\infty,\mathrm{op}}}{\mathrm{colim}}\infty-\mathrm{Cat}(X_{\bullet})([n],[\overline{m}])

\displaystyle\cong

\displaystyle\underset{[\overline{m}]\in\Delta^{\infty,\mathrm{op}}}{\mathrm{colim}}\infty-\mathrm{Cat}(X_{\bullet})([1],[\overline{m}])\times_{X_{0}}...\times_{X_{0}}\infty-\mathrm{Cat}(X_{\bullet})([1],[\overline{m}])

\displaystyle\cong

\displaystyle\underset{([\overline{m}_{i}])|_{i\in\{1,2,...,n\}}\in(\Delta^{\infty,\mathrm{op}})^{n}}{\mathrm{colim}}\infty-\mathrm{Cat}(X_{\bullet})([1],[\overline{m}_{1}])\times_{X_{0}}...\times_{X_{0}}\infty-\mathrm{Cat}(X_{\bullet})([1],[\overline{m}_{n}])

\displaystyle\cong

\displaystyle\underset{[\overline{m}_{1}]\in\Delta^{\infty,\mathrm{op}}}{\mathrm{colim}}\infty-\mathrm{Cat}(X_{\bullet})([1],[\overline{m}_{1}])\times_{X_{0}}...;\times_{X_{0}}\underset{[\overline{m}_{n}]\in\Delta^{\infty,\mathrm{op}}}{\mathrm{colim}}\infty-\mathrm{Cat}(X_{\bullet})([1],[\overline{m}_{n}])

\displaystyle\cong

\displaystyle\widetilde{L_{\mathrm{Cat}}}(X_{\bullet})_{1}\times_{\widetilde{L_{\mathrm{Cat}}}(X_{\bullet})_{0}}...\times_{\widetilde{L_{\mathrm{Cat}}}(X_{\bullet})_{0}}\widetilde{L_{\mathrm{Cat}}}(X_{\bullet})_{1},

so it follows that
\widetilde{L_{\mathrm{Cat}}}(X_{\bullet})
is a Segal space.

Proposition 3.26
.

Denote by
L_{\mathrm{Cat}}:\mathrm{Hom}_{\mathrm{Cat}}(\Delta^{\mathrm{op}},\mathscr{S})\rightarrow\mathrm{Cat}
the localization functor, then there is an isomorphism

L_{\mathrm{Cat}}\cong\widetilde{L_{\mathrm{Cat}}}.

Proof.

The functor
L_{\mathrm{Cat}}
is an idempotent monad on
\mathrm{Hom}_{\mathrm{Cat}}(\Delta^{\mathrm{op}},\mathscr{S})
. The category of algebras for an idempotent monad
L
on a category
\mathscr{C}
is a full reflective subcategory
i:\mathscr{I}\hookrightarrow\mathscr{C}
on objects of the form
Lc
, and moreover, the monad itself can be uniquely reconstructed from
\mathscr{I}
as the left adjoint to the inclusion
i
. In the argument that follows we will prove that
\widetilde{L_{\mathrm{Cat}}}
is an idempotent monad and that its category of algebras is isomorphic to
\mathrm{Cat}
, thus proving the claim in the proposition.

First, by the observations made at the end of
3.25
we have that

\widetilde{L_{\mathrm{Cat}}}\mathrm{Hom}(\Delta^{\mathrm{op}},\mathscr{S})\subset\mathrm{Cat}.

We will now prove that
\widetilde{L_{\mathrm{Cat}}}\mathscr{C}\cong\mathscr{C}
for
\mathscr{C}\in\mathrm{Cat}
. This will conclude the proof of the proposition since it would follow that
\widetilde{L_{\mathrm{Cat}}}
is idempotent and that

\mathrm{Cat}\subset\widetilde{L_{\mathrm{Cat}}}\mathrm{Hom}(\Delta^{\mathrm{op}},\mathscr{S}).

Now, observe that
\infty-\mathrm{Cat}(\mathscr{C})([1],[\overline{m}])
is an
\infty
-category whose objects are identity arrows and composable sequences

(c_{0}\xrightarrow{f_{1}}c_{1}\xrightarrow{f_{2}}...\xrightarrow{f_{m}}c_{m})

of non-unital morphisms in
\mathscr{C}
and

\mathrm{Hom}_{\infty-\mathrm{Cat}(\mathscr{C})([1],[\overline{m}])}((c_{0}\xrightarrow{f_{1}}...\xrightarrow{f_{m}}c_{m}),(c_{0}\xrightarrow{f_{1}}...\xrightarrow{f_{k}}c_{k}\xrightarrow{f_{k+s-1}\circ...\circ f_{k}}c_{k+s}\xrightarrow{f_{k+s}}...\xrightarrow{f_{m}}c_{m}))\cong\mathrm{Or}(s-2).

It follows that
\mathscr{C}_{1}
is a full subcategory of
\infty-\mathrm{Cat}(\mathscr{C})([1],[\overline{m}])
and moreover (since all
\mathrm{Or}(m)
are contractible) every object
(c_{0}\xrightarrow{f_{1}}...\xrightarrow{f_{m}}c_{m})
admits a unique-up-to-homotopy morphism to
(c_{1}\xrightarrow{f_{m}\circ...\circ f_{1}}c_{m})\in\mathscr{C}_{1}
, and so we indeed have
\widetilde{L_{\mathrm{Cat}}}\mathscr{C}\cong\mathscr{C}
.
∎

Construction 3.27
.

For a given
S\in\mathpzc{Fact}
we will denote by
\mathrm{Sq}^{\Delta}(S)
the following simplicial set: its set of
m
-simplices is the set of morphisms
[m]\times[m]\rightarrow\mathrm{Sq}(S)
in
\mathrm{Fact}
. More explicitly, it can be identified with a sequence of morphisms

x_{0}\overset{h_{1}}{\rightharpoonup}x_{1}\overset{h_{2}}{\rightharpoonup}...\overset{h_{m}}{\rightharpoonup}x_{m}\overset{v_{1}}{\rightharpoondown}x_{m+1}\overset{v_{2}}{\rightharpoondown}...\overset{v_{m}}{\rightharpoondown}x_{2m}

in
\mathrm{Sq}(S)
considered as a category with a factorization system. The degeneracy maps
\sigma_{j}
then act by sending
(h_{1},...,h_{m};v_{1},...,v_{m})
to
(h_{1},...,h_{j},\mathrm{id},...,h_{m};v_{1},...,v_{j},\mathrm{id},...,v_{m})
and
\delta_{i}
sends it to
(h_{1},...,h_{i+1}\circ h_{i},...,h_{m};v_{1},...,v_{i+1}\circ v_{i},...,v_{m})
.

Proposition 3.28
.

\mathrm{Sq}^{\Delta}(S)
are contractible simplicial sets.

Proof.

We will first prove the claim in the case
\mathrm{Sq}(S)\cong[n]\times[l]
for some
n\geq 0
and
l\geq 0
. Denote by
N([n]\times[l])
the simplicial nerve of the (ordinary) category
[n]\times[l]
. Using the fact that
[n]\times[l]
admits a factorization system, we can identify its set of
m
-simplices with the set of sequences

y_{0}\overset{\widetilde{h}_{1}}{\rightharpoonup}y_{1}\overset{\widetilde{v}_{1}}{\rightharpoondown}y_{2}\overset{\widetilde{h}_{2}}{\rightharpoonup}...\overset{\widetilde{h}_{m}}{\rightharpoonup}y_{2m-1}\overset{\widetilde{v}_{m}}{\rightharpoondown}y_{2m}

in
[n]\times[l]
. The set of such sequences can be equivalently described as the set of sequences of positive integers of the form
(0\leq i_{1}\leq...\leq i_{m}\leq n,0\leq j_{1}\leq...\leq j_{m}\leq l)
. However, it is easy to see that exactly the same data also describes an element of
\mathrm{Sq}^{\Delta}([n]\times[l])_{m}
. Moreover, an elementary inspection reveals that
\delta_{i}
and
\sigma_{j}
act the same way on
\mathrm{Sq}^{\Delta}([n]\times[l])
and
N([n]\times[l])
, so those simplicial sets are isomorphic. Finally, observe that
N([n]\times[l])
is contractible since
[n]\times[l]
admits a final object
(n,l)
.

Now assume that
S
is a general object of
\mathpzc{Fact}
. By definition there exist
n\geq 0
and
l\geq 0
such that
S
can be identified with a path from
(0,0)
to
(n,l)
in
[n]\times[l]
and
\mathrm{Sq}(S)
can be identified with the full subcategory of
[n]\times[l]
on objects lying above
S
. We will prove that the simplicial homology
H_{i}(\mathrm{Sq}^{\Delta}(S))
vanishes for
i>0
, the claim would then follow from the Hurewicz theorem. Observe that for
n
and
l
as above the simplicial chain complex
C_{\bullet}(\mathrm{Sq}^{\Delta}(S))
can be identified with a subcomplex of
C_{\bullet}(\mathrm{Sq}^{\Delta}([n]\times[l]))
. In particular, since
\mathrm{Sq}^{\Delta}([n]\times[l])
is contractible we see that for every chain
c\in C_{m}(\mathrm{Sq}^{\Delta}(S))
such that
\partial c=0
there exists
c^{\prime}\in C_{m+1}(\mathrm{Sq}^{\Delta}([n]\times[l]))
such that
\partial c^{\prime}=c
. We will temporarily denote for
k\geq 0
by
C_{k}^{+}
the subcomplex of
C_{k}(\mathrm{Sq}^{\Delta}([n]\times[l]))
on those
f:[k]\times[k]\rightarrow[n]\times[l]
whose image belongs to
\mathrm{Sq}(S)
(so
C_{k}^{+}
is isomorphic to
C_{k}(\mathrm{Sq}^{\Delta}(S))
) and by
C_{k}^{-}
the subcomplex on those
f
whose image does not belong to
\mathrm{Sq}(S)
. We then have a decomposition
c^{\prime}=c^{\prime}_{+}+c^{\prime}_{-}
with
c^{\prime}_{\pm}\in C_{m+1}^{\pm}
. Our goal now is to prove that , by possibly replacing
c^{\prime}
with
c^{\prime\prime}\overset{\mathrm{def}}{=}c^{\prime}+\partial\widetilde{c}
(so that
\partial c^{\prime\prime}=\partial c^{\prime}
), we can ensure
\partial c^{\prime\prime}_{-}=0
, since if this is true, then we can further replace
c^{\prime\prime}
with
(c^{\prime\prime}-c^{\prime\prime}_{-})\in C_{m+1}(\mathrm{Sq}^{\Delta}(S))
which will prove that
H_{m}(\mathrm{Sq}^{\Delta}(S))=0
.

We can further decompose
\partial c^{\prime}_{\pm}=(\partial c^{\prime}_{\pm})_{+}+(\partial c^{\prime}_{\pm})_{-}
. Observe that
(\partial c^{\prime}_{+})_{-}=0
. Since
c\in c_{m}^{+}
, we see that

(\partial c^{\prime})_{-}=(\partial c^{\prime}_{+})_{-}+(\partial c^{\prime}_{-})_{-}=(\partial c^{\prime}_{-})_{-}=0.

It follows that it suffices to make
(\partial c^{\prime\prime}_{-})_{+}=0
. Recall that by definition
\partial c^{\prime}_{-}=\sum_{i=0}^{m+1}\delta_{i}c^{\prime}_{-}
. Observe that, if
c^{\prime}\in C_{m+1}^{-}
, then
\delta_{i}c^{\prime}\in C_{m}^{-}
for
0<i<m+1
. Indeed, an element
f:[m]\times[m]\rightarrow[n]\times[l]
belongs to
C_{m}^{-}
if and only if
f(m,0)
lies below
S
in
[n]\times[l]
and it is easy to see that
f(m,0)=\delta_{i}f(m,0)
for
0<i<m+1
. We denote by
C^{l}_{m+1}
(resp.
C^{r}_{m+1}
) the subcomplex of
C^{-}_{m+1}
on those
a
for which
\delta_{0}a
(resp.
\delta_{m+1}a
) belongs to
C^{+}_{m}
and by
C^{l}_{m+1}(c^{\prime})
(resp.
C^{r}_{m+1}(c^{\prime})
) the set of those basis elements
a\in C^{r}_{m+1}
(resp.
a\in C^{l}_{m+1}
) that appear in
c^{\prime}
with nonzero coefficient, then we have

(\partial c^{\prime}_{-})_{+}=\sum_{a\in C^{l}_{m+1}(c^{\prime})}c_{a}\delta_{0}a+(-1)^{m+1}\sum_{a^{\prime}\in C^{r}_{m+1}(c^{\prime})}c_{a^{\prime}}\delta_{m+1}a^{\prime},

where
c_{a}\in\mathbb{Z}
are coefficients. We will finish our proof by showing that by replacing
c^{\prime}
with
c^{\prime\prime}=c^{\prime}+\partial\widetilde{c}
we can reduce the number of elements of
C^{l}_{m+1}(c^{\prime\prime})\bigcup C^{r}_{m+1}(c^{\prime\prime})
to zero. Indeed, assume that
a\in C^{l}_{m+1}(c^{\prime})
, then denote
\widetilde{a}\overset{\mathrm{def}}{=}\sigma_{0}a\in C_{m+2}([n]\times[l])
. Observe that
\delta_{0}\widetilde{a}=a
and
\delta_{i}\widetilde{a}
do not lie in
C^{l}_{m+1}
for
i>0
. It follows that for
\widehat{c}\overset{\mathrm{def}}{=}c^{\prime}-\partial(c_{a}a)
the set
C^{l}_{m+1}(\widehat{c})
has one less element than
C^{l}_{m+1}(c^{\prime})
. Also observe that if
a\notin C^{r}_{m+1}
then none of the elements
\delta_{i}\widetilde{a}
are either, so we can use the procedure above to remove elements from
C^{l}_{m+1}(c^{\prime})
without enlarging
C^{r}_{m+1}(c^{\prime})
. Finally, observe that, after we are done with
C^{l}_{m+1}(c^{\prime})
, we can reduce the size of
C^{r}_{m+1}(c^{\prime})
to zero using a very similar procedure except for setting
\widetilde{a}\overset{\mathrm{def}}{=}\sigma_{m+1}a
.
∎

Construction 3.29
.

For
m\geq 1
we will define certain
m
-categories
\qed^{\mathrm{lax}}_{m}
. We first denote by
\mathscr{W}_{m}
the walking
m
-morphism. More explicitly, we define
\mathscr{W}_{1}
to be the category
[1]
and inductively define
\mathscr{W}_{m}
to be the category with two objects
0
and
1
with no non-trivial endomorphisms such that

\mathrm{Hom}_{\mathscr{W}_{m}}(0,1)\cong\mathscr{W}_{m-1}.

We will also denote by
\mathscr{W}_{0}
the singleton category
\{0\}
.

Now we begin describing
\qed^{\mathrm{lax}}_{m}
. First, we set
\qed^{\mathrm{lax}}_{0}\overset{\mathrm{def}}{=}[1]
. We then define
\qed^{\mathrm{lax}}_{1}
to be the category with four objects
\{(i,j)\}_{i,j\in\{0,1\}}
that have no non-trivial endomorphisms and such that

\mathrm{Hom}_{\qed^{\mathrm{lax}}_{1}}((0,0),(0,1))\cong\mathrm{Hom}_{\qed^{\mathrm{lax}}_{1}}((1,0),(1,1))\cong\mathrm{Hom}_{\qed^{\mathrm{lax}}_{1}}((0,0),(1,0))\cong\mathrm{Hom}_{\qed^{\mathrm{lax}}_{1}}((0,1),(1,1))\cong\mathrm{pt}

and
\mathrm{Hom}_{\square^{\mathrm{lax}}_{1}}((0,0),(1,1))\cong[1]
. The composition operation sends the unique composable pair of morphisms from
(0,0)
to
(1,1)
passing through
(0,1)
to
\{0\}\in[1]
and the one passing through
(1,0)
to
\{1\}
. We also define the morphisms
k^{1}_{i}:\mathscr{W}_{1}\cong[1]\rightarrow\qed^{\mathrm{lax}}_{1}
for
i\in\{0,1\}
to be the inclusions of the full subcategories generated by
(i,0)
and
(i,1)
. Now assume that we have already defined
\qed^{\mathrm{lax}}_{s}
for
s<m
as well as inclusions
k^{s}_{i}:\mathscr{W}_{i}\hookrightarrow\qed^{\mathrm{lax}}_{s}
, we define
\qed^{\mathrm{lax}}_{m}
to be the
m
-category with four objects such that

\mathrm{Hom}_{\qed^{\mathrm{lax}}_{m}}((0,0),(1,0))\cong\mathrm{Hom}_{\qed^{\mathrm{lax}}_{m}}((0,1),(1,1))\cong\mathrm{pt},

\mathrm{Hom}_{\qed^{\mathrm{lax}}_{m}}((0,0),(0,1))\cong\mathrm{Hom}_{\qed^{\mathrm{lax}}_{m}}((1,0),(1,1))\cong\mathscr{W}_{m-1}

and
\mathrm{Hom}_{\qed^{\mathrm{lax}}_{m}}((0,0),(1,1))\cong\qed^{\mathrm{lax}}_{m-1}
, with the composition operators sending the upper (resp. lower) copy of
\mathscr{W}_{m-1}
into
\qed^{\mathrm{lax}}_{m-1}
by means of
k^{m-1}_{0}
(resp.
k^{m-1}_{1}
). We define
k^{m}_{0,1}:\mathscr{W}_{m}\hookrightarrow\qed^{\mathrm{lax}}_{m}
to be the inclusions of the full subcategories on
(0,0)
and
(0,1)
and on
(1,0)
and
(1,1)
.

We will also fix notation for certain natural inclusions among
\mathscr{W}_{i}
and
\qed^{\mathrm{lax}}_{j}
. Denote by
j^{1}_{k}
for
k\in\{0,1\}
the natural inclusions
\mathscr{W}_{0}\cong\{k\}\hookrightarrow\mathscr{W}_{1}\cong[1]
; we claim that it can be extended to a system of morphisms
j^{m}_{k}:\mathscr{W}_{m}\rightarrow\mathscr{W}_{m+1}
. Indeed, assuming we have already defined
j^{i<m}_{k}
, we define
j^{m}_{k}
to be identity on objects and to be given by
j^{m-1}_{k}
on the category of morphisms from
0
to
1
. Observe that we also have natural inclusions
i^{0}_{k}:\qed^{\mathrm{lax}}_{0}\hookrightarrow\qed^{\mathrm{lax}}_{1}
for
k\in\{0,1\}
sending
[1]
isomorphically onto a full subcategory of
\qed^{\mathrm{lax}}_{1}
containing
(k,0)
and
(k,1)
. Now, define
i^{m}_{k}
to be given by the identity on objects and morphisms from
(0,0)
to
(1,0)
and from
(0,1)
to
(1,1)
, by
j^{m-1}_{k}
on morphisms from
(0,0)
to
(0,1)
and from
(1,0)
to
(1,1)
and by
i^{m-1}_{k}
on morphisms from
(0,0)
to
(1,1)
. Finally, we denote by
p^{m}_{k}
the natural inclusions
[1]\hookrightarrow\qed^{\mathrm{lax}}_{m}
mapping
[1]
isomorphically onto the full subcategory generated by
(0,k)
and
(1,k)
.

Construction 3.30
.

For a given object
S\in\mathpzc{Fact}
we will temporarily denote by
\widetilde{\mathscr{L}^{\mathrm{lax}}\mathrm{Sq}(S)}
the following bicategory: its objects are the objects of
\mathrm{Sq}(S)
, a morphism from
(i,j)
to
(i^{\prime},j^{\prime})
is given by a morphism
f:\mathrm{Sq}(S^{\prime})\rightarrow\mathrm{Sq}(S)
in
\mathpzc{Fact}^{\mathrm{op}}
such that
f(0,0)=(i,j)
and
f(n^{\prime},m^{\prime})=(i^{\prime},j^{\prime})
. A 2-morphism is given by a commutative diagram

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{Sq}(S^{\prime})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{Sq}(S^{\prime\prime})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{Sq}(S)}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces f}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces s}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces g}

in
\mathpzc{Fact}^{\mathrm{op}}
in which
s
is a morphism in
\mathpzc{Fact}^{\mathrm{a.p.}}
in the notation of
2.11
.

Proposition 3.31
.

For every
S\in\mathpzc{Fact}
there is an equivalence

\widetilde{\mathscr{L}^{\mathrm{lax}}\mathrm{Sq}(S)}\cong\mathscr{L}^{\mathrm{lax}}\mathrm{Sq}(S)

Proof.

We begin by calculating
\widehat{\mathscr{L}}L^{\mathrm{lax}}\mathrm{Sq}(S)
. By definition we have

\widehat{\mathscr{L}}L^{\mathrm{lax}}\mathrm{Sq}(S)(\theta(s;\overline{t}))\cong\underset{(\theta(s;\overline{t})\rightarrow\mathrm{Sq}^{\mathrm{lax}}(S^{\prime};\overline{h};\overline{v}))\in(\theta(s;\overline{t})/\widehat{\mathscr{L}})}{\mathrm{colim}}L^{\mathrm{lax}}\mathrm{Sq}(S)(S;\overline{h};\overline{v}).

A morphism
\theta(s;\overline{t})\rightarrow\mathrm{Sq}^{\mathrm{lax}}(S^{\prime};\overline{h};\overline{v})
is given by the following data:
(s+1)
marked points
(i_{k},j_{k})|_{k\in\{0,1,...,s\}}
in
\mathrm{Sq}(S^{\prime})
,
t_{k}
paths
S_{k,l}|_{k\in\{0,1,...s-1\},l\in\{0,1,...,t_{k}-1\}}
between
(i_{k},j_{k})
and
(i_{k+1},j_{k+1})
in
\mathrm{Sq}(S^{\prime})
for every
k
,
(i_{k}-i_{k-1})
morphisms
f_{p}:[t_{k}]\rightarrow[h_{i_{k}-i_{k-1}+p}]
for
p\in\{0,1,...i_{k}-i_{k-1}-1\}
and
(j_{k}-j_{k-1})
morphisms
g_{q}:[t_{k}]\rightarrow[v_{j_{k}-j_{k-1}+q}]
for
q\in\{0,1,...j_{k}-j_{k-1}-1\}
. We will call a morphism
a:\theta(s;\overline{t})\rightarrow\mathrm{Sq}^{\mathrm{lax}}(S;\overline{h};\overline{v})
active if
i_{0}=j_{0}=0
,
i_{s}=n^{\prime}
,
j_{s}=m^{\prime}
, the paths
S_{k,0}
lie on
S^{\prime}\in\mathrm{Sq}(S^{\prime})
and all
f_{p}
and
g_{q}
are active; we will denote by
(\theta(s;\overline{t})/\widehat{\mathscr{L}})^{\mathrm{act}}
the full subcategory of
(\theta(s;\overline{t})/\widehat{\mathscr{L}})
on active morphisms. Observe that every morphism between objects in
(\theta(s;\overline{t})/\widehat{\mathscr{L}})^{\mathrm{act}}
is of the from
\widehat{\mathscr{L}}(b)
for an active morphism
b
of
\Theta{Fact}_{2}
. It follows from the above description that every morphism
f:\theta(s;\overline{t})\rightarrow\mathrm{Sq}^{\mathrm{lax}}(S^{\prime};\overline{h};\overline{v})
uniquely factors as

\theta(s;\overline{t})\overset{a}{\twoheadrightarrow}\mathrm{Sq}^{\mathrm{lax}}(S^{\prime\prime};\overline{h}^{\prime};\overline{v}^{\prime})\overset{\widehat{\mathscr{L}}(i)}{\rightarrowtail}\mathrm{lax}(S^{\prime};\overline{h};\overline{v}),

where
a
is active and
i
is an inert morphism in
(\Theta_{2}{Fact})^{\mathrm{op}}
. It follows that
(\theta(s;\overline{t})/\widehat{\mathscr{L}})^{\mathrm{act}}
is a cofinal subcategory of
(\theta(s;\overline{t})/\widehat{\mathscr{L}})
.

Observe that we have the following isomorphism

(\theta(s;\overline{t})/\widehat{\mathscr{L}})^{\mathrm{act}}\cong\overbrace{(\theta(1;t_{1})/\widehat{\mathscr{L}})^{\mathrm{act}}\times(\theta(1;t_{2})/\widehat{\mathscr{L}})^{\mathrm{act}}\times...\times(\theta(1;t_{s})/\widehat{\mathscr{L}})^{\mathrm{act}}}^{\text{$s$ times}}

obtained by sending morphisms
a_{i}:\theta(1;t_{i})\rightarrow\mathrm{Sq}^{\mathrm{lax}}(S^{\prime}_{i};\overline{h}_{i};\overline{v}_{i})
to a morphism
a:\theta(s;\overline{t})\rightarrow\mathrm{Sq}^{\mathrm{lax}}(S^{\prime};\overline{h};\overline{v})
, where
\mathrm{Sq}^{\mathrm{lax}}(S^{\prime};\overline{h};\overline{v})
is obtained by joining
\mathrm{Sq}^{\mathrm{lax}}(S^{\prime}_{i};\overline{h}_{i};\overline{v}_{i})
along the endpoints of
S_{i}
. Similarly, observe that every active morphism
a:\theta(s;\overline{t})\rightarrow\mathrm{Sq}^{\mathrm{lax}}(S^{\prime};\overline{h};\overline{v})
induces a partition of
(S^{\prime};\overline{h};\overline{v})
into segments
(S^{\prime}_{k};\overline{h}_{k};\overline{v}_{k})
from
(i_{k-1},j_{k-1})
to
(i_{k},j_{k})
for
1\leq i\leq s
. Moreover, since
L^{\mathrm{lax}}\mathrm{Sq}(S)
satisfies the Segal condition we have

L^{\mathrm{lax}}\mathrm{Sq}(S)(S^{\prime};\overline{h};\overline{v})\cong L^{\mathrm{lax}}\mathrm{Sq}(S)(S_{1}^{\prime};\overline{h}_{1};\overline{v}_{1})\times_{L^{\mathrm{lax}}\mathrm{Sq}(S)(*)}...\times_{L^{\mathrm{lax}}\mathrm{Sq}(S)(*)}L^{\mathrm{lax}}\mathrm{Sq}(S)(S_{s}^{\prime};\overline{h}_{s};\overline{v}_{s}).

Altogether we get the following string of isomorphisms:

\displaystyle\widehat{\mathscr{L}}L^{\mathrm{lax}}\mathrm{Sq}(S)(\theta(s;\overline{t}))\cong

\displaystyle\underset{(\theta(s;\overline{t})\rightarrow\mathrm{Sq}^{\mathrm{lax}}(S^{\prime};\overline{h};\overline{v}))\in(\theta(s;\overline{t})/\widehat{\mathscr{L}})}{\mathrm{colim}}L^{\mathrm{lax}}\mathrm{Sq}(S)(S;\overline{h};\overline{v})

\displaystyle\cong

\displaystyle\underset{(\theta(s;\overline{t})\rightarrow\mathrm{Sq}^{\mathrm{lax}}(S^{\prime};\overline{h};\overline{v}))\in(\theta(s;\overline{t})/\widehat{\mathscr{L}})^{\mathrm{act}}}{\mathrm{colim}}L^{\mathrm{lax}}\mathrm{Sq}(S)(S;\overline{h};\overline{v})

\displaystyle\cong

\displaystyle\underset{\bigtimes_{k=1}^{s}(\theta(1;t_{k})/\widehat{\mathscr{L}})^{\mathrm{act}}}{\mathrm{colim}}L^{\mathrm{lax}}\mathrm{Sq}(S)(S_{1}^{\prime};\overline{h}_{1};\overline{v}_{1})\times_{L^{\mathrm{lax}}\mathrm{Sq}(S)(*)}...\times_{L^{\mathrm{lax}}\mathrm{Sq}(S)(*)}L^{\mathrm{lax}}\mathrm{Sq}(S)(S_{s}^{\prime};\overline{h}_{s};\overline{v}_{s})

\displaystyle\cong

\displaystyle\underset{(\theta(1;t_{1})/\widehat{\mathscr{L}})^{\mathrm{act}}}{\mathrm{colim}}L^{\mathrm{lax}}\mathrm{Sq}(S)(S_{1}^{\prime};\overline{h}_{1};\overline{v}_{1})\times_{L^{\mathrm{lax}}\mathrm{Sq}(S)(*)}...\times_{L^{\mathrm{lax}}\mathrm{Sq}(S)(*)}\underset{(\theta(1;t_{s})/\widehat{\mathscr{L}})^{\mathrm{act}}}{\mathrm{colim}}L^{\mathrm{lax}}\mathrm{Sq}(S)(S_{s}^{\prime};\overline{h}_{s};\overline{v}_{s})

\displaystyle\cong

\displaystyle\widehat{\mathscr{L}}L^{\mathrm{lax}}\mathrm{Sq}(S)(\theta(1;t_{1}))\times_{\widehat{\mathscr{L}}L^{\mathrm{lax}}\mathrm{Sq}(S)(*)}...\times_{\widehat{\mathscr{L}}L^{\mathrm{lax}}\mathrm{Sq}(S)(*)}\widehat{\mathscr{L}}L^{\mathrm{lax}}\mathrm{Sq}(S)(\theta(1;t_{s})).

It follows that it suffice to prove the isomorphism
\widehat{\mathscr{L}}L^{\mathrm{lax}}\mathrm{Sq}(S)\cong\widetilde{\mathscr{L}^{\mathrm{lax}}\mathrm{Sq}(S)}
on objects of the form
\theta(1;t)
.

An object of
(\theta(1;t)/\widehat{\mathscr{L}})^{\mathrm{act}}
can be identified with an object
(S^{\prime};\overline{h};\overline{v})
of
\Theta_{2}{Fact}
,
t
composable permutation morphisms

S^{\prime}\overset{i_{1}}{\rightarrowtail}S^{\prime}_{1}\overset{i_{2}}{\rightarrowtail}...\overset{i_{t-1}}{\rightarrowtail}S^{\prime}_{t}

in
\mathpzc{Fact}
as well as active morphisms
a_{k}:[t]\twoheadrightarrow[h_{k}]
for
1\leq k\leq n^{\prime}
and
b_{l}:[t]\twoheadrightarrow[v_{l}]
for
1\leq l\leq m^{\prime}
. The data of active morphisms
a_{k}
and
b_{l}
can equivalently be described as an active morphism
a_{S^{\prime}}:(S^{\prime};\overline{h};\overline{v})\twoheadrightarrow q(S^{\prime},t)
for which the underlying morphism
S^{\prime}\twoheadrightarrow S^{\prime}
is an identity, where
q
is the functor described in
3.15
. Denote by
(\theta(1;t)/\widehat{\mathscr{L}})^{q}
the full subcategory of
(\theta(1;t)/\widehat{\mathscr{L}})^{\mathrm{act}}
on objects for which
a_{S^{\prime}}=\mathrm{id}
, then it follows from the description above that
(\theta(1;t)/\widehat{\mathscr{L}})^{q}
is cofinal. Explicitly, an object of
(\theta(1;t)/\widehat{\mathscr{L}})^{q}
can be identified with a string
(S^{\prime}_{1}\overset{i_{1}}{\rightarrowtail}...\overset{i_{t-2}}{\rightarrowtail}S^{\prime}_{t-1}\overset{i_{t-1}}{\rightarrowtail}S^{\prime})
of permutation morphisms, and a morphism in
(\theta(1;t)/\widehat{\mathscr{L}})^{q}
is given by a commutative diagram

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces S^{\prime\prime}_{1}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces S^{\prime\prime}_{2}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces S^{\prime\prime}_{t-1}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces S^{\prime\prime}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces S_{1}^{\prime}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces S^{\prime}_{2}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces S^{\prime}_{t-1}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces S^{\prime}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces i^{\prime}_{1}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces...}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces i^{\prime}_{t-1}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces a_{1}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces i_{1}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces...}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces a_{2}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces a_{t-1}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces i_{t-1}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces a}

in
\mathpzc{Fact}^{\mathrm{op}}
. Finally, the value of
L^{\mathrm{lax}}\mathrm{Sq}(S)
on
q(S^{\prime},t)
is given by the set of strings of morphisms

S^{\prime}\overset{a_{1}}{\twoheadrightarrow}\widetilde{S}^{\prime}_{1}\overset{a_{2}}{\twoheadrightarrow}\widetilde{S}^{\prime}_{2}\overset{a_{3}}{\twoheadrightarrow}...\overset{a_{t+1}}{\twoheadrightarrow}\widetilde{S}^{\prime}_{t+1}\xrightarrow{f}S

in
\mathpzc{Fact}^{\mathrm{op}}
. It follows that the value

\widehat{\mathscr{L}}L^{\mathrm{lax}}\mathrm{Sq}(S)(\theta(1;t))\cong\underset{(\theta(1;t)/\widehat{\mathscr{L}})^{q}}{\mathrm{colim}}L^{\mathrm{lax}}\mathrm{Sq}(S)(q(S^{\prime},t))

is given by the classifying space of the category, which we denote
\widehat{\mathscr{L}}_{S}
, whose objects are strings

S^{\prime}_{1}\overset{i_{1}}{\rightarrowtail}S^{\prime}_{2}\overset{i_{2}}{\rightarrowtail}...\overset{i_{t-1}}{\rightarrowtail}S^{\prime}\overset{a_{1}}{\twoheadrightarrow}\widetilde{S}^{\prime}_{1}\overset{a_{2}}{\twoheadrightarrow}...\overset{a_{t+1}}{\twoheadrightarrow}\widetilde{S}^{\prime}_{t+1}\xrightarrow{f}S

of morphisms in
\mathpzc{Fact}^{\mathrm{op}}
and morphisms are given by active morphisms
b:S^{\prime}\twoheadrightarrow S^{\prime\prime}
in
\mathpzc{Fact}^{\mathrm{op}}
making the following diagrams commute:

\lxSVG@closescope
\lxSVG@closescope {\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}\hss}\lxSVG@discardpath\lxSVG@closescope \hss}}\lxSVG@closescope\endpgfpicture}}.

Denote by
\widehat{\mathscr{L}}^{\mathrm{id}}_{S}
the full subcategory on those objects for which
a_{1}\cong\mathrm{id}
. Observe that
\widehat{\mathscr{L}}^{\mathrm{id}}_{S}
is both a cofinal subcategory of
\widehat{\mathscr{L}}_{S}
and a set, so it follows that the required classifying space is isomorphic to
\widehat{\mathscr{L}}^{\mathrm{id}}_{S}
. In other words, we see that
\widehat{\mathscr{L}}L^{\mathrm{lax}}\mathrm{Sq}(S)(\theta(1;t))
is given by the set of strings

(10)

\displaystyle S^{\prime}_{1}\overset{i_{1}}{\rightarrowtail}S^{\prime}_{2}\overset{i_{2}}{\rightarrowtail}...\overset{i_{t-1}}{\rightarrowtail}S^{\prime}\overset{a_{1}}{\twoheadrightarrow}\widetilde{S}^{\prime}_{2}\overset{a_{2}}{\twoheadrightarrow}...\overset{a_{t}}{\twoheadrightarrow}\widetilde{S}^{\prime}_{t}\xrightarrow{f}S

in
\mathpzc{Fact}^{\mathrm{op}}
.

Observe that
\widehat{\mathscr{L}}L^{\mathrm{lax}}\mathrm{Sq}(S)(\theta(1;t))
is
not
a Segal space, so we will have to calculate its localization

L_{\mathrm{Cat}}(\widehat{\mathscr{L}}L^{\mathrm{lax}}\mathrm{Sq}(S)(\theta(1;t))).

We will do this using
3.26
, however before that we will describe the structure of
\widehat{\mathscr{L}}L^{\mathrm{lax}}\mathrm{Sq}(S)(\theta(1;t))
as a simplicial space. Every morphism in
\Delta
can be written as a composition of
\sigma_{j}
and
\delta_{i}
, so it suffices to describe the action of these morphisms. For
0\leq j\leq t
the morphism
\sigma_{j}
sends
the string (
10
) to

S^{\prime}_{1}\overset{i_{1}}{\rightarrowtail}...\overset{i_{j}}{\rightarrowtail}S^{\prime}_{j+1}=\joinrel=S^{\prime}_{j+1}\overset{i_{j+1}}{\rightarrowtail}...\overset{i_{t-1}}{\rightarrowtail}S^{\prime}\overset{a_{1}}{\twoheadrightarrow}...\overset{a_{j}}{\twoheadrightarrow}\widetilde{S}^{\prime}_{j+1}=\joinrel=\widetilde{S}^{\prime}_{j+1}\overset{a_{j+1}}{\twoheadrightarrow}...\overset{a_{t}}{\twoheadrightarrow}\widetilde{S}^{\prime}_{t}\xrightarrow{f}S.

For
0<k<t
the morphism
\delta_{i}
sends (
10
) to

S^{\prime}_{1}\overset{i_{1}}{\rightarrowtail}...\overset{i_{k-1}}{\rightarrowtail}S^{\prime}_{k}\overset{i_{k+1}\circ i_{k}}{\rightarrowtail}S^{\prime}_{k+2}\overset{i_{k+2}}{\rightarrowtail}...\overset{i_{t-1}}{\rightarrowtail}S^{\prime}\overset{a_{1}}{\twoheadrightarrow}...\overset{a_{k-1}}{\twoheadrightarrow}\widetilde{S}^{\prime}_{k}\overset{a_{k+1}\circ a_{k}}{\twoheadrightarrow}\widetilde{S}^{\prime}_{k+2}\overset{a_{k+2}}{\twoheadrightarrow}...\overset{a_{t}}{\twoheadrightarrow}\widetilde{S}^{\prime}_{t}\xrightarrow{f}S.

It now remains to describe the action of
\delta_{t}
and
\delta_{0}
; The morphism
\delta_{0}
sends (
10
) to the string

S^{\prime\prime}_{2}\overset{i_{2}^{\prime}}{\rightarrowtail}S_{3}^{\prime\prime}\overset{i_{3}^{\prime}}{\rightarrowtail}...\overset{i_{t-1}^{\prime}}{\rightarrowtail}\widetilde{S}^{\prime}_{2}\overset{a_{2}}{\twoheadrightarrow}\widetilde{S}_{3}^{\prime}\overset{a_{3}}{\twoheadrightarrow}...\overset{a_{t}}{\twoheadrightarrow}\widetilde{S}^{\prime}_{t}\xrightarrow{f}S

appearing in the diagram

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\widetilde{S}^{\prime}_{t}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces S}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\widetilde{S}_{3}^{\prime}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces S_{1}^{\prime\prime}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces S_{2}^{\prime\prime}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces S_{3}^{\prime\prime}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\widetilde{S}^{\prime}_{2}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces S_{1}^{\prime}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces S^{\prime}_{2}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces S^{\prime}_{3}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces S^{\prime}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces f}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces...}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces i_{1}^{\prime}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces i_{2}^{\prime}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces...}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces a_{2}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces i_{1}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces i_{2}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces...}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces a_{1}}

in which all the squares at the bottom are factorization squares in
\mathpzc{Fact}^{\mathrm{op}}
. Similarly,
\delta_{t}
sends (
10
) to the string

S_{1}^{\prime}\overset{i_{1}}{\rightarrowtail}S_{2}^{\prime}\overset{i_{2}}{\rightarrowtail}...\overset{i_{t-2}}{\rightarrowtail}S^{\prime}_{t-1}\overset{a_{1}^{\prime}}{\twoheadrightarrow}\widetilde{S}^{\prime}_{2}\overset{a^{\prime}_{2}}{\twoheadrightarrow}...\overset{a^{\prime}_{t-2}}{\twoheadrightarrow}\widetilde{S}^{\prime\prime}_{t-1}\xrightarrow{f\circ a_{t-1}\circ j_{t-1}}S

appearing in the diagram

With this description we can now prove that

L_{\mathrm{Cat}}(\widehat{\mathscr{L}}L^{\mathrm{lax}}\mathrm{Sq}(S)(\theta(1;t)))([1])\cong\widetilde{\mathscr{L}^{\mathrm{lax}}\mathrm{Sq}(S)}(\theta_{1,1}).

The space
L_{\mathrm{Cat}}(\widehat{\mathscr{L}}L^{\mathrm{lax}}\mathrm{Sq}(S)(\theta(1;t)))([1])
is the classifying space of the
\infty
-category

\mathscr{L}^{\mathrm{lax}}(S)\overset{\mathrm{def}}{=}(\infty-\mathrm{Cat}(\widehat{\mathscr{L}}L^{\mathrm{lax}}\mathrm{Sq}(S)(\theta(1;t))))([1]).

The objects of
\mathscr{L}^{\mathrm{lax}}(S)
are strings of morphisms in
\mathpzc{Fact}^{\mathrm{op}}
of the form

(11)

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces S_{1}^{\prime}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces S_{2}^{\prime}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces S_{n}^{\prime}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces S}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces S_{1}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces S_{2}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces S_{n}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces S_{n+1}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces a_{1}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces...}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces a_{n}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces i_{1}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces i_{2}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces i_{n}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces f}

in which all
i_{j}
are permutations. We will denote a string like this by
((i_{1};a_{1}),(i_{2},a_{2}),...,(i_{n};a_{n}),f)
. Elementary
p
-morphisms in
\mathscr{L}^{\mathrm{lax}}(S)
are given by object in
\widehat{\mathscr{L}}L^{\mathrm{lax}}\mathrm{Sq}(S)(\theta(1;p+1))
, in particular, an elementary 1-morphism is given by a diagram

{}{}}
{{}{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}}{{}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}{}{{}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces
}{{{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@transformcm{1.0}{0.0}{0.0}{1.0}{107.82448pt}{-30.04012pt}\lxSVG@begingroup@{transform} \pgfsys@hbox{64}\lxSVG@closescope }}}

\lxSVG@closescope
\lxSVG@closescope {\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}\hss}\lxSVG@discardpath\lxSVG@closescope \hss}}\lxSVG@closescope\endpgfpicture}},

the target of this morphism is then the string
((i_{3}\circ i_{1};a_{2}\circ a_{3}),f)
and the source is the string
((i_{1};a_{1}),(i_{2};a_{2}),f)
. We will denote this morphism by
((i_{2},i_{1};a_{2},a_{3}),f)
and we will more generally denote by

((i_{n+1},i_{n},...,i_{1};a_{n+1},a_{n},...a_{1}),f)

an elementary
n
-morphism represented by the string

(S_{0}\overset{i_{1}}{\rightarrowtail}S_{1}\overset{i_{2}}{\rightarrowtail}...\overset{i_{n+1}}{\rightarrowtail}S_{n+1}\overset{a_{1}}{\twoheadrightarrow}S_{n+2}\overset{a_{2}}{\twoheadrightarrow}...\overset{a_{n+1}}{\twoheadrightarrow}S_{2n+2}\xrightarrow{f}S).

We will sometimes omit the morphism
f
from the notation if it is obvious from the context.

Before moving on, we need to introduce some notation. By
2.11
the morphisms in
\mathpzc{Fact}
given by a composition of a permutation and an active morphism form a subcategory
\mathpzc{Fact}^{\mathrm{a.p.}}
. This category admits an obvious factorization system (given by permutations and active morphisms), and so we can view it as a functor
\mathpzc{Fact}^{\mathrm{a.p.}}:\mathpzc{Fact}\rightarrow\mathscr{S}
. An elementary
n
-morphism in
\mathscr{L}^{\mathrm{lax}}(S)
can then be identified with a pair
(s,g)
consisting of an element
s\in\mathpzc{Fact}^{\mathrm{a.p.}}([n+1]\times[n+1])
and a morphism
g:s(n+1,n+1)\rightarrow S
. Now, assume that we have a morphism
h:\mathrm{Sq}(S)\rightarrow[n+1]\times[n+1]
in
\mathrm{Fact}
, where we assume that
S\subset[l]\times[k]
is a string with
k
horizontal and
l
vertical morphisms, then we will denote by
h^{*}(s,f)
the pair consisting of an element
h^{*}s\in\mathpzc{Fact}^{\mathrm{a.p.}}(S)
together with a morphism

h^{*}g:h^{*}s(k,l)\cong s(h(k),h(l))\xrightarrow{p}s(n+1,n+1)\xrightarrow{g}S,

in which
p
is a morphism given by composing all morphism in
s
along some path between
h(k,l)
and
(n+1,n+1)
in
[n+1]\times[n+1]
.

We will now define a morphism

F:\mathscr{L}^{\mathrm{lax}}(S)\rightarrow\widetilde{\mathscr{L}^{\mathrm{lax}}(S)}\overset{\mathrm{def}}{=}\infty-\mathrm{Cat}(\widetilde{\mathscr{L}^{\mathrm{lax}}\mathrm{Sq}(S)}(\theta(1;a)))([1]).

The objects of
\widetilde{\mathscr{L}^{\mathrm{lax}}(S)}
are formal compositions of morphisms in
\widetilde{\mathscr{L}^{\mathrm{lax}}(\mathrm{Sq}(S))}
, and those are of the form
S_{0}\overset{a}{\twoheadrightarrow}S_{1}\overset{i}{\rightarrowtail}S_{2}\xrightarrow{f}S
. Given an object
x\overset{\mathrm{def}}{=}((i_{1};a_{1}),...,(i_{l},a_{l}),f)
of
\mathscr{L}^{\mathrm{lax}}(S)
, we define
F(x)
to be the composition of
f
and
\widetilde{i}_{k}\circ\widetilde{a}_{k}
, where
\widetilde{i}_{k}\circ\widetilde{a_{k}}
is the factorization of
a_{k}\circ i_{k}
in
\mathpzc{Fact}^{\mathrm{a.p.}}
. The elementary
n
-morphisms of
\widetilde{\mathscr{L}^{\mathrm{lax}}(S)}
are given by strings

S_{0}\overset{a_{1}}{\twoheadrightarrow}S_{1}\overset{i_{1}}{\rightarrowtail}S_{2}\overset{a_{2}}{\twoheadrightarrow}S_{3}...\overset{i_{n+1}}{\rightarrowtail}S_{2n+2}\xrightarrow{f}S

of morphisms in
\mathpzc{Fact}^{\mathrm{op}}
in which all
i_{j}
are permutation. Thus an elementary
n
-morphism in
\widetilde{\mathscr{L}^{\mathrm{lax}}(S)}
can be identified with a pair
(s,f)
, where
s
is an element of
\mathpzc{Fact}^{\mathrm{a.p.}}(S^{\prime}_{n+1})
, where
S^{\prime}_{n+1}
is a string of the form

\overbrace{vhvh...vh}^{\text{$(n+1)$ times}}

and
f:S_{2n}\rightarrow S
is a morphism in
\mathpzc{Fact}
. To define the required morphism
F
it suffices to give its values on elementary morphisms. Observe that there is a natural morphism

\gamma:\mathrm{Sq}(S^{\prime}_{n+1})\rightarrow[n+1]\times[n+1]

in
\mathrm{Fact}
, and we denote the required morphism by sending an elementary
n
-morphism in
\mathscr{L}^{\mathrm{lax}}(S)
of the form
(s,g)
with
s\in\mathpzc{Fact}^{\mathrm{a.p.}}([n]\times[n])
to the elementary morphism in
\widetilde{\mathscr{L}^{\mathrm{lax}}(S)}
given by
\gamma^{*}(s,g)
.

We will now construct a morphism
G:N(\widetilde{\mathscr{L}^{\mathrm{lax}}(S)})\rightarrow N(\mathscr{L}^{\mathrm{lax}}(S))
, where
N(-)
denotes the classifying space of an
\infty
-category. Once again, it suffices to define it on objects and elementary morphisms. We first define it on objects: an object of
\widetilde{\mathscr{L}^{\mathrm{lax}}(S)}
is a formal composition of the form
f\circ(i_{l}\circ a_{l})\circ(i_{l-1}\circ a_{l-1})\circ...\circ(i_{1}\circ a_{1})
; the functor
G
then sends it to the object
((\mathrm{id};a_{1}),(i_{1};\mathrm{id}),...,(\mathrm{id};i_{l}),(a_{l};\mathrm{id}),f)
of
\mathscr{L}^{\mathrm{lax}}(S)
. Before we describe the action of
G
on morphisms, we make the following observation: given an elementary
n
-morphism in
\widetilde{\mathscr{L}^{\mathrm{lax}}(S)}
corresponding to a pair
(s,f)
as above, to every morphism
h:[m+1]\times[m+1]\rightarrow S^{\prime}_{n+1}
we can associate
h^{*}(s,f)
, which we can identify with an elementary
m
-morphism in
\mathscr{L}^{\mathrm{lax}}(S)
. It follows that to every elementary
n
-morphism (which we can consider as a morphism
[n]\xrightarrow{(s,f)}N(\widetilde{\mathscr{L}^{\mathrm{lax}}(S)})
) we can associate a morphism
\beta_{(s,f)}:\mathrm{Sq}^{\Delta}(S^{\prime}_{n})\rightarrow N(\mathscr{L}^{\mathrm{lax}}(S))
. Now assume that we are given an elementary
n
-morphism in
\widetilde{\mathscr{L}^{\mathrm{lax}}(S)}
corresponding to the string
((i_{1};a_{1}),...,(i_{n+1},a_{n+1}),f)
. Denote by
S^{\prime\prime}_{n+1}
the following string

\overbrace{hh....h}^{\text{$(n+1)$ times}}\overbrace{vv....v}^{\text{$(n+1)$ times}}

considered as an object of
\mathpzc{Fact}
. Observe that there is a natural morphism

b:\mathrm{Sq}(S^{\prime\prime}_{n+1})\rightarrow\mathrm{Sq}(S^{\prime}_{n+1})

in
\mathrm{Fact}
given by the inclusion of the top left corner of
\mathrm{Sq}(S^{\prime}_{n+1})
. Assume that the value of
b^{*}s
is given by the string
i^{\prime}_{n+1}\circ i^{\prime}_{n}\circ...i^{\prime}_{m}\circ a^{\prime}_{n+1}\circ...\circ a^{\prime}_{1}
. We now define
G(s,f)
to be given by the following string of morphisms in
N(\mathscr{L}^{\mathrm{lax}}(S))
:

\displaystyle((\mathrm{id};a_{1}),(i_{1};\mathrm{id}),(\mathrm{id};a_{2})...,(\mathrm{id};a_{n+1}),(i_{n+1};\mathrm{id}),f)\xrightarrow{(i_{1},\mathrm{id};\mathrm{id},a_{1})*\mathrm{id}}((\mathrm{id};a_{1}),(i_{1};a_{2}),...,(a_{l};\mathrm{id}),(\mathrm{id};i_{l}),f)\rightarrow

\displaystyle\xrightarrow{((\mathrm{id},i_{1};a_{2},\mathrm{id})*\mathrm{id})^{-1}}((\mathrm{id};a_{1}),(\mathrm{id};\widetilde{a}_{2}),(\widetilde{i}_{1};\mathrm{id}),(\mathrm{id};a_{3}),...,f)\xrightarrow{(\widetilde{i}_{1},\mathrm{id};\mathrm{id},a_{3})*\mathrm{id}}((\mathrm{id};a_{1}),(\mathrm{id};\widetilde{a}_{2}),(\widetilde{i}_{1};a_{3}),...,f)\xrightarrow{...}

\displaystyle\xrightarrow{...}((\mathrm{id};a^{\prime}_{1}),...,(\mathrm{id};a^{\prime}_{n+1}),(i^{\prime}_{1};\mathrm{id}),...,(i^{\prime}_{n+1};\mathrm{id}),f)\xrightarrow{(\mathrm{id},...,\mathrm{id};a^{\prime}_{1},...,a^{\prime}_{n+1})*(i^{\prime}_{1},...,i^{\prime}_{n+1};\mathrm{id},...,\mathrm{id})}((a^{\prime}_{n+1}\circ...\circ a^{\prime}_{1};

\displaystyle i^{\prime}_{n+1}\circ...\circ i^{\prime}_{1}),f),

where
a_{2}\circ i_{1}\cong\widetilde{i}_{1}\circ\widetilde{a}_{2}
is the active/inert factorization in
\mathpzc{Fact}^{\mathrm{a.p.}}
. Before moving on, we need to prove that it actually defines a functor. In order to do this it suffices to prove that for every elementary
n
-morphism
(s,f)
as above and
0\leq q<n
we have

d_{q}^{\pm}(G(s,f))\cong G(d_{q}^{\pm}(s,f)).

To do this, observe that by their definition both
d_{q}^{\pm}(G(s,f))
and
G(d_{q}^{\pm}(s,f))
are
q
-morphisms in the image of
\beta_{(s,f)}:\mathrm{Sq}^{\Delta}(S^{\prime}_{n})\rightarrow N(\mathscr{L}^{\mathrm{lax}}(S))
. Since by
3.28
all
\mathrm{Sq}^{\Delta}(S^{\prime}_{n})
are contractible, we see that all
q
-morphisms in this image are isomorphic.

Observe that
FG
sends an object of
N(\widetilde{\mathscr{L}^{\mathrm{lax}}(S)})
of the form
((a_{1};i_{1}),...,(a_{l};i_{l}),f)
to

((a_{1};\mathrm{id}),(\mathrm{id};i_{1}),...,(a_{l};\mathrm{id}),(\mathrm{id};i_{l}),f).

Since
N(\widetilde{\mathscr{L}^{\mathrm{lax}}(S)})\cong\widetilde{\mathscr{L}^{\mathrm{lax}}(\mathrm{Sq}(S))}(\theta_{1,1})
, we see that
FG\cong\mathrm{id}
. We will now provide a homotopy
\eta:GF\xrightarrow{\sim}\mathrm{id}
. More specifically, we will provide the following data:

•

For every object
x\in\mathscr{L}^{\mathrm{lax}}(S)
a morphism
\eta_{x}:x\rightarrow GFx
;

•

For every (possibly non-commutative) diagram

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces x}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces y}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces GFx}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces y}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(\epsilon)}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\eta_{x}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(GF\epsilon)}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\eta_{y}}

in which
\epsilon
is an
n
-morphism a morphism
\alpha_{\epsilon}:\qed^{\mathrm{lax}}_{n}\rightarrow N(\mathscr{L}^{\mathrm{lax}}(S))
such that
\alpha_{\epsilon}\circ p^{n}_{0,1}\cong\eta_{x,y}
,
\alpha_{\epsilon}\circ k^{n}_{0}\cong\epsilon
,
\alpha_{\epsilon}\circ k^{n}_{1}\cong GF\epsilon
and
\alpha_{\epsilon}\circ i^{n}_{0,1}\cong\alpha_{d^{\pm}_{n-1}\epsilon}
in the notation of
3.29
;

such that
\alpha_{\bullet}
is additionally compatible with the compositions of
n
-morphisms. Now, since the
n
-morphisms of
\mathscr{L}^{\mathrm{lax}}(S)
are freely generated under composition by elementary morphisms, it suffices to provide
\alpha_{\epsilon}
only for elementary morphisms
\epsilon
.

We first describe the 1-morphisms
\eta_{x}
. First, assume that
x
is represented by a morphism
((i;a),f)
in
\infty-\mathrm{Cat}(\widehat{\mathscr{L}}L^{\mathrm{lax}}\mathrm{Sq}(S)(\theta(1;a)))
, in that case we set

\eta_{x}\overset{\mathrm{def}}{=}((\mathrm{id},i;a,\mathrm{id}),f).

The target of this morphism is
x
and the source is the string
((\mathrm{id};\widetilde{a})(\widetilde{i};\mathrm{id}),f)
, where
\widetilde{i}\circ\widetilde{a}\cong a\circ i
is the active/inert decomposition in
\mathpzc{Fact}^{\mathrm{a.p.}}
, which is equal to
GFx
. For a more general object of the form
y\overset{\mathrm{def}}{=}((i_{1};a_{1}),...,(i_{l},a_{l}),f)
we set

\eta_{y}\overset{\mathrm{def}}{=}\eta_{(i_{1};a_{1})}*\eta_{(i_{2};a_{2})}*...*\eta_{i_{l};a_{l}}.

Before we move on to constructing
\alpha_{\epsilon}
we make the following observation: to every morphism elementary
n
-morphism
(s^{\prime},g)
in
\mathscr{L}^{\mathrm{lax}}(S)
we can associate a morphism
\gamma_{(s^{\prime},g)}:\mathrm{Sq}^{\Delta}([n+1]\times[n+1])\rightarrow N(\mathscr{L}^{\mathrm{lax}}(S))
in the same manner we have constructed
\beta_{(s,f)}
earlier. Now, we will construct the required morphisms
\alpha_{\epsilon}
by induction on the dimension of
\epsilon
, starting with the case of a
1
-morphism. Assume that
\epsilon
is an elementary 1-morphism given by
((i_{1},i_{2};a_{1},a_{2}),f)
, moreover assume that we have the following factorization diagram in
\mathpzc{Fact}^{\mathrm{a.p.}}
:

However, observe that all the morphisms in this diagram are lie in the image of
\mathrm{Sq}^{\Delta}([2]\times[2])
under
\gamma_{((i_{1},i_{2};a_{1},a_{2}),f)}
. Since
\mathrm{Sq}^{\Delta}([2]\times[2])
is contractible by
3.28
, there is a
2
-morphism connecting the two composites in the above diagram moreover it may be chosen to lie in the image of
\gamma_{((i_{1},i_{2};a_{1},a_{2}),f)}
, we define
\alpha_{(i_{1},i_{2};a_{1},a_{2}),f}
to be this 2-morphism. Now assume that we have already defined
\alpha_{\epsilon}
for all elementary morphisms of dimension
<n
, and moreover that all
\alpha_{\epsilon}
lie in the image of
\gamma_{\epsilon}
. Assume we are given an elementary
n
-morphism
\epsilon_{n}
. In this case we see that
\alpha_{d^{\pm}_{p}\epsilon_{n}}
for
0\leq p<n
collectively define a morphism
\alpha_{\partial\epsilon_{n}}:\partial\qed^{\mathrm{lax}}_{n}\rightarrow N(\mathscr{L}^{\mathrm{lax}}(S))
and moreover by our assumptions it factors as

\hbox to196.95pt{\vbox to76.95pt{\pgfpicture\makeatletter\hbox{\hskip 98.47281pt\lower-36.30952pt\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke} \lxSVG@begingroup@{fill} \lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\nullfont\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} {}{}{}{{}}\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\hbox{\hbox{{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} {{}{}{{{}}{{}}{{}}{{}}}{{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}}{{}{}{{
