Finally, observe that since
\mathrm{Sq}^{\Delta}([n+1]\times[n+1])
is contractible and
\partial\qed^{\mathrm{lax}}_{n}
is homotopy equivalent to an
n
-sphere we see that
\alpha_{\partial\epsilon_{n}}
extends to
\alpha_{\epsilon_{n}}:\qed^{\mathrm{lax}}_{n}\rightarrow N(\mathscr{L}^{\mathrm{lax}}(S))
and moreover it factors through
\gamma_{\epsilon_{n}}
.
∎

Definition 3.32
.

Assume we are given monads
T_{1,2}:\mathrm{B}\Delta_{a}\rightarrow\mathscr{B}
. Observe that there are natural inclusions
i_{1,2}:\mathrm{B}\Delta\rightarrow\mathrm{B}\Delta_{a}\otimes\mathrm{B}\Delta_{a}
given by sending
[1]
to
[1]\otimes[0]
and
[0]\otimes[1]
respectively. We say that a morphism
D:\mathrm{B}\Delta_{a}\otimes\mathrm{B}\Delta_{a}\rightarrow\mathscr{B}
is a
distributive law
between
T_{1}
and
T_{2}
if
D\circ i_{1}\cong T_{1}
and
D\circ i_{2}\cong T_{2}
.

Remark 3.33
.

Using the description of
L^{\mathrm{lax}}\mathscr{F}
in
3.20
and the equivalence
\Delta^{\mathrm{act},\mathrm{op}}\cong\Delta_{a}
of
[
Kos21
, Lemma 4.29.]
, we see that
L^{\mathrm{lax}}*\cong\mathrm{B}\Delta_{a}\times\mathrm{B}\Delta_{a}
. It follows that
\mathrm{B}\Delta_{a}\otimes\mathrm{B}\Delta_{a}\cong\mathscr{L}^{\mathrm{lax}}*
, so a distributive law in a bicategory
\mathscr{B}
can e equivalently described as a lax functor from
*
viewed as a category with a factorization system. Using
3.31
we see that
\mathscr{L}^{\mathrm{lax}}*
is a category with a single object
*
whose category of endomorphisms is equivalent to
\mathpzc{Fact}^{\mathrm{a.p.}}
with the monoidal structure given by concatenation of strings. Observe that the morphism
t:\mathpzc{Cat}\rightarrow\mathpzc{Fact}
of
2.17
induces a functor
t^{\mathrm{act}}:\mathpzc{Cat}^{\mathrm{act}}\rightarrow\mathpzc{Fact}^{\mathrm{a.p.}}
. Once again using the equivalence
\Delta^{\mathrm{act},\mathrm{op}}\cong\Delta_{a}
we see that
t^{\mathrm{act}}
induces a functor
\mathrm{B}\Delta_{a}\rightarrow\mathscr{L}^{\mathrm{lax}}*\cong\mathrm{B}\Delta_{a}\otimes\mathrm{B}\Delta_{a}
. Assuming we have a distributive law
D:\mathrm{B}\Delta_{a}\otimes\mathrm{B}\Delta_{a}\rightarrow\mathscr{B}
we see that
t
sends the morphism
[1]
in
\mathrm{B}\Delta_{a}
to
T_{1}T_{2}
. It follows that, if we are given a distributive law between monads
T_{1}
and
T_{2}
, their composition also admits a structure of a monad.

Remark 3.34
.

Using
3.10
, we see that giving a distributive law
D
in
\mathscr{B}
is equivalent to giving a monad in the category of monads. This is the characterization of distributive laws used for example in section 6 of
[
Str72a
]
.

4.
Completeness for categories with factorization systems

The goal of this section is to develop the notion of a groupoid object in
\mathrm{Fact}
and a complete object in
\mathrm{Fact}
, similarly to the corresponding notions in
\mathrm{Cat}
. We define these concepts in
definition

4.10
. We prove that the inclusion
\mathrm{Fact}^{\mathrm{Gpd}}\hookrightarrow\mathrm{Fact}
admits a right adjoint in
4.13
, which is analogous to the ”maximal subgroupoid” functor in
\mathrm{Cat}
. However, most of this section is dedicated to constructing an auxiliary theory
\mathpzc{Fact}^{\overset{\sim}{\rightarrow}}
and describing its category of models. The theory
\mathpzc{Fact}^{\overset{\sim}{\rightarrow}}
is defined in
4.2
. Its category of models
\mathrm{Fact}^{\overset{\sim}{\rightarrow}}
turns out to be isomorphic to a category of morphisms
(F:\mathscr{G}\rightarrow\mathscr{F})
in
\mathrm{Fact}
that induce an isomorphism on the space of objects, which is proved in
4.8
and
4.9
. Finally, we use this category to construct a completion functor for categories with factorization systems in
4.14
.

Lemma 4.1
.

For an arbitrary active morphism
a:S\twoheadrightarrow S^{\prime}
and a surjective active morphism
s:S\twoheadrightarrow S^{\prime\prime}
in
\mathpzc{Fact}^{\mathrm{op}}_{/\mathscr{F}}
there exists an object
S_{0}\in\mathpzc{Fact}^{\mathrm{op}}
fitting in the following pushout diagram

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces S}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces S^{\prime}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces S^{\prime\prime}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces S_{0}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces a}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces s}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces p}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces b}

in
\mathpzc{Fact}^{\mathrm{op}}
.

Proof.

Since
s
is a surjective morphism, the string
S^{\prime\prime}
is obtained from
S
by contracting some of its elementary segments. Since
a
is an active morphism, every elementary segment of
S^{\prime}
is in the image of a unique elementary segment of
S
. We define
p:S^{\prime}\twoheadrightarrow S_{0}
to be the surjective morphism that contracts all elementary segments that lie in the image of an elementary segment contracted by
s
. This also uniquely determines the morphism
b
: indeed, since
s
is surjective, every elementary segment of
S^{\prime\prime}
is in the image of a unique elementary segment of
S
, and we define
b
to send an elementary segment of
S^{\prime\prime}
to the image under
p\circ a
of its preimage in
S
.

To prove that this is indeed a pushout, observe that a cocone over this pushout diagram can be identified with a morphism
f:S^{\prime}\rightarrow S_{1}
in
\mathpzc{Fact}^{\mathrm{op}}
such that if
e\in S
is an elementary segment such that
s(e)
is an identity morphism and
a(e)\cong e^{\prime}_{n}\circ e^{\prime}_{n-1}\circ...\circ e^{\prime}_{1}
, then
f(e^{\prime}_{n}\circ e^{\prime}_{n-1}\circ...\circ e^{\prime}_{1})\cong\mathrm{id}
. However, this is only possible if all
e^{\prime}_{i}
map to identities, so
f
must factor through
S_{0}
.
∎

Warning
.

The pushout described in
lemma

4.1
generally does not remain a pushout in
\mathrm{Fact}
.

Construction 4.2
.

We denote by
\mathpzc{Fact}^{\overset{\sim}{\rightarrow}}
the opposite of the following category: its objects can be identified with morphisms
\mathrm{Sq}(S^{\prime})\overset{s}{\twoheadrightarrow}\mathrm{Sq}(S)
in which
s
is a surjective active morphism and a morphism from
(s_{0},f_{0})
to
(s_{1},f_{1})
is given by a commutative diagram of the form

(12)

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{Sq}(S_{0})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{Sq}(S_{1})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{Sq}(S^{\prime}_{0})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{Sq}(S^{\prime}_{1})}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces s_{0}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces h}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces s_{1}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces h^{\prime}}

in
\mathrm{Fact}
, such that
h
additionally satisfies the following conditions:

•

if
x_{0}\in S^{\prime}_{0}
is such that
h^{\prime}(x_{0})\notin S^{\prime}_{1}\subset\mathrm{Sq}(S^{\prime}_{1})
, then for every elementary segment
i:e\rightarrowtail S_{0}
such that
s_{0}\circ i=\mathrm{id}_{x_{0}}
the morphism
h\circ i
is a constant functor;

•

if
h^{\prime}(x_{0})\in S_{1}^{\prime}
, then the following condition is satisfied: assume that
i:e\rightarrowtail S_{0}
is an elementary segment of
S_{0}
such that
s_{0}\circ i=\mathrm{id}_{x_{0}}
, denote by
h\circ i\cong j\circ c\circ a
the unique decomposition in which
a
is active,
c
is a covering morphism and
j
is an inclusion. Then the image of
s_{1}\circ j
in
\mathrm{Sq}(S^{\prime}_{1})
is equal to
\{h^{\prime}(x_{0})\}
.

We will call a morphism inert (resp. an inclusion) if the morphism
h
is inert (resp. an inclusion) and denote the corresponding subcategory of
\mathpzc{Fact}^{\overset{\sim}{\rightarrow}}
by
(\mathpzc{Fact}^{\overset{\sim}{\rightarrow}})^{\mathrm{int}}
(resp.
(\mathpzc{Fact}^{\overset{\sim}{\rightarrow}})^{\mathrm{incl}}
). We will denote by
k:(\mathpzc{Fact}^{\overset{\sim}{\rightarrow}})^{\mathrm{int}}\hookrightarrow\mathpzc{Fact}^{\overset{\sim}{\rightarrow}}
and
j:(\mathpzc{Fact}^{\overset{\sim}{\rightarrow}})^{\mathrm{incl}}\hookrightarrow(\mathpzc{Fact}^{\overset{\sim}{\rightarrow}})^{\mathrm{int}}
the inclusions of the corresponding subcategories. We will call a morphism
(h,h^{\prime})
active if
h
is an active morphism in
\mathpzc{Fact}^{\mathrm{op}}
and the commutative diagram (
12
) is a pushout square.

We will declare an object elementary if it either has the from
e\twoheadrightarrow*
or
e=\joinrel=e
for an elementary object
e
of
\mathpzc{Fact}
, we will denote by
i:(\mathpzc{Fact}^{\overset{\sim}{\rightarrow}})^{\mathrm{el}}\hookrightarrow(\mathpzc{Fact}^{\overset{\sim}{\rightarrow}})^{\mathrm{incl}}
the full subcategory of
(\mathpzc{Fact}^{\overset{\sim}{\rightarrow}})^{\mathrm{incl}}
on elementary objects. For an object
s
of
\mathpzc{Fact}^{\overset{\sim}{\rightarrow}}
we will denote by
(\mathpzc{Fact}^{\overset{\sim}{\rightarrow}})^{\mathrm{el}}_{s/}
the category
s/i
. Denote by
\mathscr{E}_{\mathpzc{Fact}^{\overset{\sim}{\rightarrow}}}
the full subcategory of
\mathrm{Hom}_{\mathrm{Cat}}((\mathpzc{Fact}^{\overset{\sim}{\rightarrow}})^{\mathrm{int}},\mathscr{S})
on those functors
\mathscr{G}:(\mathpzc{Fact}^{\overset{\sim}{\rightarrow}})^{\mathrm{int}}\rightarrow\mathscr{S}
for which

j*\mathscr{G}\cong i_{*}i^{*}j^{*}\mathscr{G}.

Remark 4.3
.

To an object
s_{0}
of
\mathpzc{Fact}^{\overset{\sim}{\rightarrow}}
we can associate a pair consisting of the object
\mathrm{Sq}(S_{0})
of
\mathrm{Fact}
and the factorization subcategory
\mathscr{I}_{s_{0}}\hookrightarrow\mathrm{Sq}(S_{0})
that contains all objects of
\mathrm{Sq}(S_{0})
, but only those morphisms that are given by factorizations and compositions of elementary morphisms
e\in S_{0}
that are sent to identity morphisms by
s_{0}
. A morphism
h:\mathrm{Sq}(S_{0})\rightarrow\mathrm{Sq}(S_{1})
as in the diagram above obtains a structure of a morphism in
\mathpzc{Fact}^{\overset{\sim}{\rightarrow}}
if and only if it takes
\mathscr{I}_{s_{0}}
to
\mathscr{I}_{s_{1}}
.

Proposition 4.4
.

The active and inert morphisms form a factorization system on
\mathpzc{Fact}^{\overset{\sim}{\rightarrow}}
.

Proof.

Assume that the morphism
h
in diagram
12
has the active/inert factorization of the form
(a,i)
. By
lemma

4.1
we then have the following commutative diagram in
\mathpzc{Fact}^{\mathrm{op}}

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{Sq}(S_{0})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{Sq}(S_{a})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{Sq}(S_{1})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{Sq}(S^{\prime}_{0})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{Sq}(S^{\prime}_{a})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{Sq}(S^{\prime}_{1})}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces s_{0}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces a}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces i}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces s_{a}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces s_{1}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces a^{\prime}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces i^{\prime}}

in which the left square is a pushout diagram and the morphism
i^{\prime}
is obtained by the universal property of the pushout from
(s_{1}\circ i,h^{\prime})
. We now need to prove that the morphisms given by this diagram actually lie in
\mathpzc{Fact}^{\overset{\sim}{\rightarrow}}
. Since an active morphism either takes an elementary morphism to an identity morphism or to a composition of elementary morphisms, we see that the conditions of
4.2
are satisfied for the left square in the diagram, so it remains to prove that the right square represents a morphism in
\mathpzc{Fact}^{\overset{\sim}{\rightarrow}}
. Recall that

\mathrm{Act}{Fact}(S)\cong\prod_{e\in S}\mathrm{Act}{Fact}(e),

where the product runs over all elementary segments of
S
. The morphism
a^{\prime}
corresponds to the element
(a_{s_{0}^{-1}(e_{1})},a_{s_{0}^{-1}(e_{2})},...,a_{s_{0}^{-1}(e_{m})})
of
\mathrm{Act}{Fact}(S^{\prime}_{0})
, where
s_{0}^{-1}(e_{j})
is the unique elementary segment of
S_{0}
that maps to
e_{i}\in S^{\prime}_{0}
(which exists since
s_{0}
is surjective) and
a_{s_{0}^{-1}(e_{j})}
is an element of
\mathrm{Act}{Fact}(e_{j})
corresponding to
a
. Assume that
x\in S^{\prime}_{a}
does not lie in the image of
a^{\prime}
. In this case the fiber
s_{a}^{-1}(x)=*
, so the conditions of
4.2
are satisfied automatically. Now assume that
x=a(x_{0})
. Denote by
S_{x_{0}}
the substring of
S_{0}
on those elementary segments that are sent to
x_{0}
by
s_{0}
and by
a_{x_{0}}:\mathrm{Sq}(S_{x_{0}})\twoheadrightarrow\mathrm{Sq}(S_{a,x_{0}})
the active morphism corresponding to the restriction of
a
to
S_{x_{0}}
, then the elementary segment of
S_{a}
lying in
s_{a}^{-1}(x)
are given by the elementary segments of
S_{a,x_{0}}
. However, all of them are in the image under
a_{x_{0}}
of the elementary segments in
S_{x_{0}}
, and it is easy to see that the conditions of
4.2
are satisfied for them since they are satisfied for the elementary morphisms in
S_{x_{0}}
by assumption. It follows that the right square indeed corresponds to a morphism in
\mathpzc{Fact}^{\overset{\sim}{\rightarrow}}
and the whole diagram provides the required factorization. The fact that it is unique follows from the uniqueness of the active/inert factorization in
\mathpzc{Fact}
and the universal property of the pushout.
∎

Lemma 4.5
.

For an object
s\in\mathpzc{Fact}^{\overset{\sim}{\rightarrow}}
denote by
(\mathpzc{Fact}^{\overset{\sim}{\rightarrow}})^{\mathrm{el}}_{0,s/}
the full subcategory of
(\mathpzc{Fact}^{\overset{\sim}{\rightarrow}})^{\mathrm{el}}_{s/}
on those morphisms
s\overset{(i,i^{\prime})}{\rightarrowtail}e
for which both
i
and
i^{\prime}
are inert in
\mathpzc{Fact}
. Then
(\mathpzc{Fact}^{\overset{\sim}{\rightarrow}})^{\mathrm{el}}_{0,s/}
is coinitial in
(\mathpzc{Fact}^{\overset{\sim}{\rightarrow}})^{\mathrm{el}}_{s/}
.

Proof.

Indeed, by an easy inspection the only objects of
(\mathpzc{Fact}^{\overset{\sim}{\rightarrow}})^{\mathrm{el}}_{s/}
that do
not
belong to
(\mathpzc{Fact}^{\overset{\sim}{\rightarrow}})^{\mathrm{el}}_{0,s/}
are given by diagrams of the form

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces e}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{Sq}(S)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces e}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{Sq}(S^{\prime})}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces i}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces s}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\{x\}}

in
\mathpzc{Fact}^{\mathrm{op}}
in which the top row is an inclusion of an elementary segment of
S
that is sent to an object
x\in\mathrm{Sq}(S^{\prime})
by the surjective morphism
s
. However, observe that this morphism uniquely factors as

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces e}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces e}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{Sq}(S)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces e}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces*}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{Sq}(S^{\prime}),}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces i}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces p}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces s}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces p}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\{x\}}

where
p
is the unique surjective morphism from
e
to
*
. This concludes the proof since both morphisms in this diagram are inert in
\mathpzc{Fact}^{\overset{\sim}{\rightarrow}}
and the right square obviously belongs to
(\mathpzc{Fact}^{\overset{\sim}{\rightarrow}})^{\mathrm{el}}_{0,s/}
.
∎

Proposition 4.6
.

The subcategory
\mathscr{E}_{\mathpzc{Fact}^{\overset{\sim}{\rightarrow}}}
endows
k:(\mathpzc{Fact}^{\overset{\sim}{\rightarrow}})^{\mathrm{int}}\hookrightarrow\mathpzc{Fact}^{\overset{\sim}{\rightarrow}}
with the structure of a theory with arities.

Proof.

We need to prove that the monad
k_{!}k^{*}
on
\mathrm{Hom}_{\mathrm{Cat}}((\mathpzc{Fact}^{\overset{\sim}{\rightarrow}})^{\mathrm{int}},\mathscr{S})
preserves the image of
\mathscr{E}_{\mathpzc{Fact}^{\overset{\sim}{\rightarrow}}}
. We first prove that for an object
s:\mathrm{Sq}(S)\twoheadrightarrow\mathrm{Sq}(S^{\prime})
of
\mathpzc{Fact}^{\overset{\sim}{\rightarrow}}
we have

\mathrm{Act}_{\mathpzc{Fact}^{\overset{\sim}{\rightarrow}}}(s)\cong\underset{(s\rightarrowtail e)\in(\mathpzc{Fact}^{\overset{\sim}{\rightarrow}})^{\mathrm{el}}_{0,s/}}{\lim}\mathrm{Act}_{\mathpzc{Fact}^{\overset{\sim}{\rightarrow}}}(e).

To do this, we first make the structure of
(\mathpzc{Fact}^{\overset{\sim}{\rightarrow}})^{\mathrm{el}}_{0,s/}
more explicit: its objects are can be identified with pairs
(j:e\rightarrowtail sq(S),i)
, where
e
is an elementary object of
\mathpzc{Fact}
,
j
is an inert morphisms in
\mathpzc{Fact}^{\mathrm{op}}
and
i\in\{0,1\}
is equal to
0
if
s\circ j
sends
e
to a single point and to
1
if
s\circ j
is an inert morphism. Morphisms between objects of
(\mathpzc{Fact}^{\overset{\sim}{\rightarrow}})^{\mathrm{el}}_{0,s/}
are given by morphisms in
\mathpzc{Fact}^{\mathrm{el}}_{S/}
. Now observe that by definition
\mathrm{Act}_{\mathpzc{Fact}^{\overset{\sim}{\rightarrow}}}(s)\cong\mathrm{Act}{Fact}(S)
, and so we have

\displaystyle\mathrm{Act}_{\mathpzc{Fact}^{\overset{\sim}{\rightarrow}}}(s)\cong

\displaystyle\mathrm{Act}{Fact}(S)

\displaystyle\cong

\displaystyle\underset{(j:S\rightarrowtail e)\in\mathpzc{Fact}^{\mathrm{el}}_{S/}}{\lim}\mathrm{Act}{Fact}(e)

\displaystyle\cong

\displaystyle\underset{(j:S\rightarrowtail e,i)\in\mathpzc{Fact}^{\mathrm{el}}_{0,S/}}{\lim}\mathrm{Act}{Fact}(e)

\displaystyle\cong

\displaystyle\underset{(j:S\rightarrowtail e,i)\in\mathpzc{Fact}^{\mathrm{el}}_{0,S/}}{\lim}\mathrm{Act}_{\mathpzc{Fact}^{\overset{\sim}{\rightarrow}}}(e)

\displaystyle\cong\cong

\displaystyle\underset{(j:s\rightarrowtail e)\in\mathpzc{Fact}^{\mathrm{el}}_{S/}}{\lim}\mathrm{Act}_{\mathpzc{Fact}^{\overset{\sim}{\rightarrow}}}(e),

in which the last isomorphism follows from
lemma

4.5
.

We will now prove that for any factorization square

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces s_{0}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces s_{1}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces i_{!}s_{0}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces e}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces a}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces a^{*}i}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces i}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces i_{!}a}

in
\mathpzc{Fact}^{\overset{\sim}{\rightarrow}}
we have

(\mathpzc{Fact}^{\overset{\sim}{\rightarrow}})^{\mathrm{el}}_{0,s_{0}/}\cong\underset{i\in(\mathpzc{Fact}^{\overset{\sim}{\rightarrow}})^{\mathrm{el}}_{0,s_{1}/}}{\lim}(\mathpzc{Fact}^{\overset{\sim}{\rightarrow}})^{\mathrm{el}}_{0,i_{!}s_{0}/}.

Observe that
(\mathpzc{Fact}^{\overset{\sim}{\rightarrow}})^{\mathrm{el}}_{0,i_{!}s_{0}/}
is a full subcategory of
(\mathpzc{Fact}^{\overset{\sim}{\rightarrow}})^{\mathrm{el}}_{0,s_{0}/}
on those elementary segments that lie in the image of
e
under
a
(considered as a morphism in
(\mathpzc{Fact}^{\overset{\sim}{\rightarrow}})^{\mathrm{op}}
). Since
a
is an active morphism, we see that those images cover
s_{0}
and only possibly intersect along boundary points, which easily implies our claim. Now we can finish the proof of the proposition in exactly the same way as in
2.9
.
∎

Construction 4.7
.

We will endow the category
[1]\times\mathpzc{Fact}
with the structure of a theory as follows: we declare
([1]\times\mathpzc{Fact})^{\mathrm{int}}\overset{\mathrm{def}}{=}[1]\times\mathpzc{Fact}^{\mathrm{int}}
,
([1]\times\mathpzc{Fact})^{\mathrm{incl}}\overset{\mathrm{def}}{=}[1]\times\mathpzc{Fact}^{\mathrm{incl}}
and
([1]\times\mathpzc{Fact})^{\mathrm{act}}\overset{\mathrm{def}}{=}\mathpzc{Fact}^{\mathrm{act}}\coprod\mathpzc{Fact}^{\mathrm{act}}
. We declare an object
(i,e)\in[1]\times\mathpzc{Fact}
elementary if
e
is an elementary object of
\mathpzc{Fact}
. Finally, we define
\mathscr{E}_{[1]\times\mathpzc{Fact}}
to be the full subcategory of
\mathrm{Hom}_{\mathrm{Cat}}([1]\times\mathpzc{Fact}^{\mathrm{int}},\mathscr{S})
on those functors whose restriction to
([1]\times\mathpzc{Fact})^{\mathrm{incl}}
is the right Kan extension of their restriction to
([1]\times\mathpzc{Fact})^{\mathrm{el}}
.

We define
\widehat{\mathpzc{Fact}^{\overset{\sim}{\rightarrow}}}
to be the full subcategory of
([1]\times\mathpzc{Fact})
containing all objects except
(0,*)
. We define the structure of a theory on
\widehat{\mathpzc{Fact}^{\overset{\sim}{\rightarrow}}}
to be the one induced from
[1]\times\mathpzc{Fact}
.

Proposition 4.8
.

The category of models for
\widehat{\mathpzc{Fact}^{\overset{\sim}{\rightarrow}}}
is isomorphic to the category of triples
(\mathscr{G},\mathscr{F},F:\mathscr{G}\rightarrow\mathscr{F})
, where
\mathscr{F}
and
\mathscr{G}
are objects of
\mathrm{Fact}
and
F
induces an isomorphism on the space of objects.

Proof.

It follows easily from the definition that the category of models for
[1]\times\mathpzc{Fact}
is the category
[1]\times\mathrm{Fact}
of triples
(\mathscr{G},\mathscr{F},F:\mathscr{G}\rightarrow\mathscr{F})
(without any additional assumptions on
F
). We will denote by
\widehat{i}:\widehat{\mathpzc{Fact}^{\overset{\sim}{\rightarrow}}}\hookrightarrow[1]\times\mathpzc{Fact}
the natural inclusion. Since
\widehat{i}
is an inclusion of a full subcategory,

\widehat{i}_{*}:\mathrm{Hom}_{\mathrm{Cat}}(\widehat{\mathpzc{Fact}^{\overset{\sim}{\rightarrow}}},\mathscr{S})\rightarrow\mathrm{Hom}_{\mathrm{Cat}}([1]\times\mathpzc{Fact},\mathscr{S})

induces an isomorphism between
\mathrm{Hom}_{\mathrm{Cat}}(\widehat{\mathpzc{Fact}^{\overset{\sim}{\rightarrow}}},\mathscr{S})
and the full subcategory of
\mathrm{Hom}_{\mathrm{Cat}}([1]\times\mathpzc{Fact},\mathscr{S})
on those
\mathscr{F}
for which
\widehat{i}_{*}\widehat{i}^{*}\mathscr{F}\cong\mathscr{F}
.

It now suffices to prove that
\widehat{i}_{*}
induces a functor between the corresponding categories of models. Indeed, it is easy to see that for
\widehat{\mathscr{F}}\in\mathrm{Hom}_{\mathrm{Cat}}(\widehat{\mathpzc{Fact}^{\overset{\sim}{\rightarrow}}},\mathscr{S})
we have
\widehat{i}_{*}\widehat{\mathscr{F}}(i,S)\cong\widehat{\mathscr{F}}(i,S)
for
(i,S)\neq(0,*)
and
\widehat{i}_{*}\widehat{\mathscr{F}}(0,*)\cong\widehat{\mathscr{F}}(1,*)
, so in particular
\widehat{i}_{*}\widehat{\mathscr{F}}\in[1]\times\mathrm{Fact}
. It follows that
\widehat{\mathrm{Fact}^{\overset{\sim}{\rightarrow}}}
is isomorphic to the full subcategory of
[1]\times\mathrm{Fact}
on those objects
X\overset{\mathrm{def}}{=}(\mathscr{G},\mathscr{F},F:\mathscr{G}\rightarrow\mathscr{F})
for which
F
induces an isomorphism between
X(0,*)\cong\mathscr{F}_{0}
and
X(1,*)\cong\mathscr{G}_{0}
, which is exactly what we needed to prove.
∎

Proposition 4.9
.

The category of model for
\mathpzc{Fact}^{\overset{\sim}{\rightarrow}}
and
\widehat{\mathpzc{Fact}^{\overset{\sim}{\rightarrow}}}
are isomorphic.

Proof.

We denote by
j:\widehat{\mathpzc{Fact}^{\overset{\sim}{\rightarrow}}}\hookrightarrow\mathpzc{Fact}^{\overset{\sim}{\rightarrow}}
the functor that sends
(0,S)
to the unique morphism
S\rightarrow*
,
(1,S)
to
\mathrm{id}:S=\joinrel=S
and sends the natural morphism
(0,S)\rightarrow(1,S)
to the diagram

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces S}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces S}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces S}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces*}

in
\mathpzc{Fact}
. Observe that
j
is an inclusion of a full subcategory. Moreover, it respects elementary objects, active and inert morphisms, and inclusions. We will prove that
j_{*}
induces an isomorphism between the categories of models.

To do this we first need to show that it restricts to
j_{*}:\widehat{\mathrm{Fact}^{\overset{\sim}{\rightarrow}}}\rightarrow\mathrm{Fact}^{\overset{\sim}{\rightarrow}}
. Observe that

\mathrm{Act}_{\widehat{\mathpzc{Fact}^{\overset{\sim}{\rightarrow}}}}(i,S)\cong\mathrm{Act}_{\mathpzc{Fact}^{\overset{\sim}{\rightarrow}}}(j(i,S))\cong\mathrm{Act}_{\mathpzc{Fact}}(S).

It follows that
j
uniquely lifts active morphisms. In particular, if for a given
s\in\mathpzc{Fact}^{\overset{\sim}{\rightarrow}}
we denote by
(s/j)^{\mathrm{int}}\hookrightarrow(s/j)
the full subcategory on inert morphisms
s\rightarrowtail j(x)
, then it is coinitial. Moreover, observe that any inert morphism
s\overset{k}{\rightarrowtail}j(x)
admits a further unique factorization of the form
j(c)\circ i
, where
i
is an inclusion in
\mathpzc{Fact}^{\overset{\sim}{\rightarrow}}
and
c
is a covering morphism. Namely, for a morphism
s\overset{k^{\prime}}{\rightarrowtail}j(S^{\prime}_{0},1)
given by the diagram

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{Sq}(S^{\prime}_{0})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{Sq}(S)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{Sq}(S^{\prime}_{0})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{Sq}(S^{\prime})}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces k^{\prime}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces s}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces s\circ k^{\prime}}

the factorization is given by

\hbox to239.57pt{\vbox to72.3pt{\pgfpicture\makeatletter\hbox{\hskip 119.78479pt\lower-35.33733pt\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke} \lxSVG@begingroup@{fill} \lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\nullfont\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} {}{}{}{{}}\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\hbox{\hbox{{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} {{}{}{{{}}{{}}{{}}{{}}{{}}{{}}}{{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}}{{}{}{{

in which the top row is the factorization of
2.14
.
It is obvious that in both cases this factorization is unique. It follows that the inclusion
(s/j)^{\mathrm{incl}}\hookrightarrow(s/j)^{\mathrm{int}}
of the full subcategory on inclusions is also a coinitial morphism. Using this we compute for
\widehat{\mathscr{F}}\in\widehat{\mathrm{Fact}^{\overset{\sim}{\rightarrow}}}

\displaystyle j_{*}\widehat{\mathscr{F}}(s)\cong

\displaystyle\underset{(s\xrightarrow{f}j(y))\in(s/j)}{\lim}\widehat{\mathscr{F}}(y)

\displaystyle\cong

\displaystyle\underset{(s\overset{i}{\rightarrowtail}j(x))\in(s/j)^{\mathrm{incl}}}{\lim}\widehat{\mathscr{F}}(x)

\displaystyle\cong

\displaystyle\underset{(s\overset{i}{\rightarrowtail}j(x))\in(s/j)^{\mathrm{incl}}}{\lim}\underset{(x\overset{k}{\rightarrowtail}e)\in(\widehat{\mathpzc{Fact}^{\overset{\sim}{\rightarrow}}})^{\mathrm{el}}_{x/}}{\lim}\widehat{\mathscr{F}}(e)

\displaystyle\cong

\displaystyle\underset{(s\overset{i}{\rightarrowtail}e)\in(\mathpzc{Fact}^{\overset{\sim}{\rightarrow}})^{\mathrm{el}}_{s/}}{\lim}\underset{(e\overset{k}{\rightarrowtail}j(x))\in(e/j)^{\mathrm{incl}}}{\lim}\widehat{\mathscr{F}}(x)

\displaystyle\cong

\displaystyle\underset{(s\overset{i}{\rightarrowtail}e)\in(\mathpzc{Fact}^{\overset{\sim}{\rightarrow}})^{\mathrm{el}}_{s/}}{\lim}j_{*}\widehat{\mathscr{F}}(e),

in which the first isomorphism follows by definition, the second by the coinitiality of
(s/j)^{\mathrm{incl}}
, the third since
\widehat{\mathscr{F}}
is a model, the fourth by the naturality of right Kan extensions and the existence of a commutative diagram

\lxSVG@closescope {
{}{}{}}{}{

{{{{{}}{

\lxSVG@closescope
\lxSVG@closescope {\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}\hss}\lxSVG@discardpath\lxSVG@closescope \hss}}\lxSVG@closescope\endpgfpicture}}.

It follows that
j_{*}
takes
\widehat{\mathrm{Fact}^{\overset{\sim}{\rightarrow}}}
to
\mathrm{Fact}^{\overset{\sim}{\rightarrow}}
. Since
j
is an inclusion of a full subcategory, we have
j^{*}j_{*}\cong\mathrm{id}
. It remains to prove that
j_{*}j^{*}\cong\mathrm{id}
. To do this, observe that since every elementary object is in the image of
j
we have
j_{*}j^{*}\mathscr{F}(e)\cong\mathscr{F}(e)
for every
\mathscr{F}\in\mathrm{Fact}^{\overset{\sim}{\rightarrow}}
. However, since both
\mathscr{F}
and
j_{*}j^{*}\mathscr{F}(e)
satisfy the Segal condition, it follows that they are isomorphic.
∎

Definition 4.10
.

For a non-negative integer
n
we will denote by
c_{n}
the category given by the string of
n
invertible morphisms. We can also describe it as a category with
(n+1)
objects such that for any pair of objects
(i,j)
in
c_{n}
we have
\mathrm{Hom}_{c_{n}}(i,j)\cong*
. For every
n,m\geq 0
we will consider
c_{m}\times c_{n}
as an object of
\mathrm{Fact}
using
remark

2.21
. Observe that there is a morphism
i:t([1])\rightarrow c_{1}\times c_{1}
given by the composition of inclusions

t([1])\rightarrow[1]\times[1]\rightarrow c_{1}\times c_{1}.

We will call an object
\mathscr{F}\in\mathrm{Fact}
a
groupoid
if every morphism
t([1])\rightarrow\mathscr{F}
uniquely factors through
i
. We will call it
complete
if every morphism
c_{1}\times c_{1}\rightarrow\mathscr{F}
factors through
*
. We will denote by
\mathrm{Fact}^{\mathrm{comp}}
the full subcategory of
\mathrm{Fact}
on complete objects and by
\mathrm{Fact}^{\mathrm{Gpd}}
the full subcategory of
\mathrm{Fact}
on groupoids.

Proposition 4.11
.

An object
\mathscr{F}
of
\mathrm{Fact}
is a groupoid if and only if its underlying category
t^{*}\mathscr{F}
is a groupoid.

Proof.

If
\mathscr{F}
is a groupoid, then the fact that
t^{*}\mathscr{F}
is a groupoid follows immediately from the definition. Assume that
t^{*}\mathscr{F}
is a groupoid, we need to show that every
f:t([1])\rightarrow\mathscr{F}
factors through
c_{1}\times c_{1}
. The morphism
f
is essentially given by a composable pair
h\circ v
of a vertical and a horizontal morphism in
\mathscr{F}
. Since
t^{*}\mathscr{F}
is a groupoid, both
h
and
v
have inverses. Let
v^{-1}\circ h^{-1}\cong\widetilde{h}^{-1}\circ\widetilde{v}^{-1}
be the horizontal/vertical factorization in
\mathscr{F}
. Observe that we have
h\circ v\cong\widetilde{v}\circ\widetilde{h}
since both morphisms are inverse to
v^{-1}\circ h^{-1}
and any two inverses are isomorphic. It follows that
(v,h,\widetilde{v},\widetilde{h})
define the required morphism
c_{1}\times c_{1}\rightarrow\mathscr{F}
.
∎

Construction 4.12
.

Denote by
\mathpzc{Fact}^{\mathrm{Gpd}}
the opposite of full subcategory of
\mathrm{Fact}
on objects of the form
c_{n}\times c_{m}
. For an object
S\in\mathpzc{Fact}
such that
S
is a longest composable string of morphisms in
[n]\times[m]
denote by
j(S)
the category
c_{n}\times c_{m}
. Observe that
j
extends to a functor from
\mathpzc{Fact}
to
\mathpzc{Fact}^{\mathrm{Gpd}}
. Indeed, by the same reasoning as in
4.11
we see that every morphism in
j(S)
is obtained from taking factorizations of morphisms of
S
, taking inverses to those factorizations or by taking factorizations of those inverses. All those operations are preserved by morphisms in
\mathrm{Fact}
, so it follows that a morphism
f:j(S)\rightarrow c_{l}\times c_{t}
is uniquely determined by its restriction to
S
. With this in mind, for a morphism
f:\mathrm{Sq}(S)\rightarrow\mathrm{Sq}(S^{\prime})
we define
j(f)
to correspond to the composition

S\hookrightarrow\mathrm{Sq}(S)\xrightarrow{f}\mathrm{Sq}(S^{\prime})\hookrightarrow j(S^{\prime}).

We denote by
\widetilde{\mathrm{Fact}^{\mathrm{Gpd}}}
the full subcategory of
\mathrm{Hom}_{\mathrm{Cat}}(\mathpzc{Fact}^{\mathrm{Gpd}},\mathscr{S})
on those functors
\mathscr{G}
that satisfy the following condition: for every maximal composable string
S\subset[n]\times[m]
we have

\mathscr{G}(c_{n}\times c_{m})\cong\underset{(S\rightarrowtail e)\in\mathpzc{Fact}^{\mathrm{el}}_{S/}}{\lim}\mathscr{G}(j(e)).

Proposition 4.13
.

The category
\widetilde{\mathrm{Fact}^{\mathrm{Gpd}}}
of
4.12
is isomorphic to
\mathrm{Fact}^{\mathrm{Gpd}}
. Moreover, the natural inclusion
i:\mathrm{Fact}^{\mathrm{Gpd}}\hookrightarrow\mathrm{Fact}
admits a right adjoint.

Proof.

Observe that by the definition of
\widetilde{\mathrm{Fact}^{\mathrm{Gpd}}}
the morphism
J^{*}
induces a morphism
j^{*}:\widetilde{\mathrm{Fact}^{\mathrm{Gpd}}}\rightarrow\mathrm{Fact}
. We first prove that its right adjoint also restricts to a functor
j_{*};\mathrm{Fact}\rightarrow\widetilde{\mathrm{Fact}^{\mathrm{Gpd}}}
. To do this, first observe that every morphism
\mathrm{Sq}(S)\rightarrow c_{n}\times c_{m}
uniquely factors through
j(S)
. Using this fact we see that

\displaystyle j_{*}\mathscr{F}(c_{n}\times c_{m})\cong

\displaystyle\underset{(j(S)\rightarrow c_{n}\times c_{m})}{\lim}\mathscr{F}(S)

\displaystyle\cong

\displaystyle\underset{(\mathrm{Sq}(S)\rightarrow c_{n}\times c_{m})}{\lim}\mathscr{F}(S)

\displaystyle\cong

\displaystyle\underset{(\mathrm{Sq}(S)\rightarrow c_{n}\times c_{m})}{\lim}\mathrm{Hom}_{\mathrm{Fact}}(\mathrm{Sq}(S),\mathscr{F})

\displaystyle\cong

\displaystyle\mathrm{Hom}_{\mathrm{Fact}}(\underset{(\mathrm{Sq}(S)\rightarrow c_{n}\times c_{m})}{\mathrm{colim}}\mathrm{Sq}(S),\mathscr{F})

\displaystyle\cong

\displaystyle\mathrm{Hom}_{\mathrm{Fact}}(c_{n}\times c_{m},\mathscr{F}).

We have already observed in
4.12
that any morphism
j(S)\rightarrow\mathscr{F}
is uniquely determined by its restriction to
S
. Since every maximal composable chain
S\in[n]\times[m]
induces an isomorphism
j(S)\cong c_{n}\times c_{m}
and a morphism from
S
is uniquely determined by its restriction to the elementary segments we see that

j_{*}\mathscr{F}(c_{n}\times c_{m})\cong\mathrm{Hom}_{\mathrm{Fact}}(c_{n}\times c_{m},\mathscr{F})\cong\underset{(S\rightarrowtail e)\in\mathpzc{Fact}^{\mathrm{el}}_{S/}}{\lim}\mathrm{Hom}_{\mathrm{Fact}}(j(e),\mathscr{F})\cong\underset{(S\rightarrowtail e)\in\mathpzc{Fact}^{\mathrm{el}}_{S/}}{\lim}j_{*}\mathscr{F}(j(e)).

We now demonstrate that
j^{*}j_{*}
is an idempotent comonad on
\mathrm{Fact}
. It suffices to prove that
(j^{*}j_{*})^{2}\mathscr{F}(e)\cong j^{*}j_{*}\mathscr{F}(e)
for all
e\in\mathpzc{Fact}^{\mathrm{el}}
. The case of
e=*
is trivial since
j^{*}j_{*}\mathscr{F}(*)\cong\mathscr{F}(*)
. The space
j^{*}j_{*}\mathscr{F}(h)
(resp.
j^{*}j_{*}\mathscr{F}(v)
) is the space of invertible morphisms in the horizontal category
h^{*}\mathscr{F}
(resp. vertical category
v^{*}\mathscr{F}
), and so the fact that
(j^{*}j_{*})^{2}\mathscr{F}(h)\cong j^{*}j_{*}\mathscr{F}(h)
(resp.
(j^{*}j_{*})^{2}\mathscr{F}(v)\cong j^{*}j_{*}\mathscr{F}(v)
) follows since the maximal subgroupoid of a groupoid is equivalent to the groupoid itself.

The adjunction
j^{*}\dashv j_{*}
is moreover comonadic. Indeed, it follows since
j^{*}
preserves all limits and is conservative and
[
Lur
, Theorem 4.7.3.5.]
. To finish the proof, observe that the category of coalgebras for
j^{*}j_{*}
is the category of
\mathscr{F}\in\mathrm{Fact}
for which
j^{*}j_{*}\mathscr{F}\cong\mathscr{F}
. In other words, of those
\mathscr{F}
for which every morphism
\mathrm{Sq}(S)\rightarrow\mathscr{F}
uniquely factors through
j(S)
. It is easy to see that this is the same category as
\mathrm{Fact}^{\mathrm{Gpd}}
, which proves our first claim, and the required right adjoint is then given by
j_{*}
.
∎

Corollary 4.14
.

The inclusion
\mathrm{Fact}^{\mathrm{comp}}\hookrightarrow\mathrm{Fact}
admits a left adjoint.

Proof.

Denote by
p:\mathpzc{Fact}^{\overset{\sim}{\rightarrow}}\rightarrow\mathpzc{Fact}
the morphism that sends
\mathrm{Sq}(S)\overset{s}{\twoheadrightarrow}\mathrm{Sq}(S)
to
\mathrm{Sq}(S)
. It is easy to see that
p^{*}
induces a morphism
p^{*}:\mathrm{Fact}\rightarrow\mathrm{Fact}^{\overset{\sim}{\rightarrow}}
. Namely, under the identifications of
4.8
and
4.9
, the morphism
p^{*}
sends
\mathscr{F}
to the pair
(\mathscr{F}_{0}\hookrightarrow\mathscr{F})
, where
\mathscr{F}_{0}
denotes the space of objects of
\mathscr{F}
. Observe that it has a left adjoint given by
Lp_{!}
, where
L
is the left adjoint to inclusion
\mathrm{Fact}\hookrightarrow\mathrm{Hom}_{\mathrm{Cat}}(\mathpzc{Fact},\mathscr{S})
.

Observe that the counit morphism
(j^{*}j_{*}\mathscr{F}\rightarrow\mathscr{F})
is an element of
\mathrm{Fact}^{\overset{\sim}{\rightarrow}}
. We claim that

\widehat{\mathscr{F}}\overset{\mathrm{def}}{=}Lp_{!}(j^{*}j_{*}\mathscr{F}\rightarrow\mathscr{F})

is the required left adjoint. Indeed, a morphism
\widehat{\mathscr{F}}\rightarrow\mathscr{G}
is the same thing as a morphism
f:\mathscr{F}\rightarrow\mathscr{G}
such that the composition
j^{*}j_{*}\mathscr{F}\rightarrow\mathscr{F}\xrightarrow{f}\mathscr{G}
factors through
\mathscr{G}_{0}
. However, since
\mathscr{G}
belongs to
\mathrm{Fact}^{\mathrm{comp}}
, every morphism from a groupoid to
\mathscr{G}
factors through
\mathscr{G}_{0}
, so
\mathrm{Hom}_{\mathrm{Fact}}(\widehat{\mathscr{F}},\mathscr{G})\cong\mathrm{Hom}_{\mathrm{Fact}}(\mathscr{F},\mathscr{G})
.
∎

Remark 4.15
.

It is not true in general that the underlying category of
\widehat{\mathscr{F}}
is complete. This is because
j^{*}j_{*}\mathscr{F}
only contains invertible morphisms for which both the horizontal and vertical components of the factorization are also invertible, but there can be invertible morphisms in
t^{*}\mathscr{F}
for which this is not the case. However, the converse is true: if the underlying category of
\mathscr{F}
is complete, then
\mathscr{F}
itself is also complete as an object of
\mathrm{Fact}
.

5.
Lax functors to span

This section contains our second main result,
theorem

5.7
, that establishes an equivalence between categories with factorization systems over
\mathscr{F}
and lax functors to
\mathrm{Span}
. To prove it, we first construct a theory
\mathpzc{Fact}_{/}\mathscr{F}
in
5.2
whose category of models is isomorphic to
\mathrm{Fact}_{/\mathscr{F}}
. We then characterize the category of lax functors from
\mathrm{Sq}(S)
to
\mathrm{Span}
as a subcategory of a certain presheaf category in
5.6
. Finally, we provide an equivalence between
\mathrm{Fact}_{/\mathscr{F}}
and that category in
theorem

5.7
. As a corollary, we can characterize the category
\mathrm{Fact}
itself as a category of distributive laws in
\mathrm{Span}
.

Definition 5.1
.

Assume we have a theory
\mathscr{T}
with arities
\mathscr{E}\subset\mathrm{Hom}_{\mathrm{Cat}}(\mathscr{T}_{0},\mathscr{S})
together with a model
X
. To
X
, viewed as a functor
X:\mathscr{T}_{1}\rightarrow\mathscr{S}
, we can associate a left fibration
p:X_{1}\rightarrow\mathscr{T}_{1}
, we also denote by
X_{0}
its restriction to
\mathscr{T}_{0}
. Denote by
\mathscr{E}_{X}
the full subcategory of
\mathrm{Hom}_{\mathrm{Cat}}(X_{0},\mathscr{S})
on those objects
\mathscr{F}
for which
p_{!}\mathscr{F}\in\mathscr{E}
, this gives
(X_{0}\rightarrow X_{1})
the structure of a theory which we denote
\mathscr{T}_{/X}
. Observe that under the equivalence

\mathrm{Hom}_{\mathrm{Cat}}(X_{1},\mathscr{S})\cong\mathrm{Hom}_{\mathrm{Cat}}(\mathscr{T}_{1},\mathscr{S})_{/X}

of
[
Kos21
, Proposition 3.12.]
the category of models for
\mathscr{T}_{/X}
can be associated with the overcategory
\mathrm{Mod}_{\mathscr{T}}(\mathscr{S})_{/X}
.

Construction 5.2
.

Assume we are given an object
\mathscr{F}\in\mathrm{Fact}
. We can associate to it a left fibration
\mathpzc{Fact}_{/\mathscr{F}}\rightarrow\mathpzc{Fact}
as well as its restriction
\mathpzc{Fact}_{/\mathscr{F}}^{\mathrm{act}}
(resp.
\mathpzc{Fact}_{/\mathscr{F}}^{\mathrm{int}}
,
\mathpzc{Fact}_{/\mathscr{F}}^{\mathrm{incl}}
,
\mathpzc{Fact}_{/\mathscr{F}}^{\mathrm{el}}
) to
\mathrm{Fact}^{\mathrm{act}}
(resp.
\mathrm{Fact}^{\mathrm{int}}
,
\mathrm{Fact}^{\mathrm{incl}}
,
\mathrm{Fact}^{\mathrm{el}}
), denote by
i_{\mathscr{F}}:\mathpzc{Fact}_{/\mathscr{F}}^{\mathrm{el}}\hookrightarrow\mathpzc{Fact}_{/\mathscr{F}}^{\mathrm{incl}}
and
j_{\mathscr{F}}:\mathpzc{Fact}_{/\mathscr{F}}^{\mathrm{incl}}\hookrightarrow\mathpzc{Fact}_{/\mathscr{F}}^{\mathrm{int}}
the natural inclusions. We endow it with the structure of a theory by declaring
\mathscr{E}_{\mathpzc{Fact}_{/\mathscr{F}}}
to be the full subcategory on those
\mathscr{G}:\mathpzc{Fact}_{/\mathscr{F}}^{\mathrm{int}}\rightarrow\mathscr{S}
whose restriction to
\mathpzc{Fact}_{/\mathscr{F}}^{\mathrm{incl}}
is the right Kan extension of its restriction to
\mathpzc{Fact}_{/\mathscr{F}}^{\mathrm{el}}
.

Proposition 5.3
.

The category of models for
\mathpzc{Fact}_{/\mathscr{F}}
is equivalent to
\mathrm{Fact}_{/\mathscr{F}}
.

Proof.

We need to prove that for a morphism
(\mathscr{G}\xrightarrow{f}\mathscr{F})
of presheaves on
\mathrm{Fact}^{\mathrm{op}}
the presheaf
\mathscr{G}
is an object of
\mathrm{Fact}
if and only if
f
viewed as a functor
\mathpzc{Fact}_{/\mathscr{F}}\rightarrow\mathscr{S}
belongs to
\mathrm{Mod}_{\mathpzc{Fact}_{/\mathscr{F}}}(\mathscr{S})
. First, assume that
\mathscr{G}
is an object of
\mathrm{Fact}
and fix
S\in\mathrm{Fact}
and
x\in\mathscr{F}(S)
. Then we have the following pullback square

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathscr{G}_{x}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathscr{G}(S)\cong\underset{\mathpzc{Fact}^{\mathrm{el}}_{S/}}{\lim}\mathscr{G}(e)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\underset{\mathpzc{Fact}^{\mathrm{el}}_{S/}}{\lim}*\cong*}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathscr{F}(S)\cong\underset{\mathpzc{Fact}^{\mathrm{el}}_{S/}}{\lim}\mathscr{F}(e)}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces f}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces x}

and the claim follows from the commutativity of limits. Now assume that
i_{\mathscr{F},*}i^{*}_{\mathscr{F}}j^{*}_{\mathscr{F}}f\cong j^{*}_{\mathscr{F}}f
. Observe that by
[
CH19
, Corollary 7.17]
and the reasoning in
[
Kos21
, Proposition 2.1.]
we have the following commutative diagram

\lxSVG@closescope
\lxSVG@closescope {\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}\hss}\lxSVG@discardpath\lxSVG@closescope \hss}}\lxSVG@closescope\endpgfpicture}},

and so it follows that

i_{*}i^{*}j^{*}p^{\mathrm{int}}_{!}f\cong i_{*}i^{*}p^{\mathrm{incl}}_{!}j^{*}_{\mathscr{F}}f\cong i_{*}p^{\mathrm{el}}_{!}i^{*}_{\mathscr{F}}j^{*}_{\mathscr{F}}f\cong p^{\mathrm{int}}_{!}i_{\mathscr{F},*}i^{*}_{\mathscr{F}}j^{*}_{\mathscr{F}}f\cong p^{\mathrm{incl}}_{!}j^{*}_{\mathscr{F}}f\cong j^{*}p^{\mathrm{int}}_{!}f,

where the first two and the last equivalence follow from the Beck-Chevalley isomorphism.
∎

Corollary 5.4
.

For
\mathscr{F}\in\mathrm{Fact}
we have an equivalence

\mathrm{Fact}_{/\mathscr{F}}\cong\underset{(\mathrm{Sq}(S)\xrightarrow{f}\mathscr{F})\in\mathpzc{Fact}_{/\mathscr{F}}}{\lim}\mathrm{Fact}_{/\mathrm{Sq}(S)}.

Proof.

Indeed, since
\mathscr{P}(\mathpzc{Fact}^{\mathrm{op}})
is an
\infty
-topos and
\mathscr{F}\cong\underset{(\mathrm{Sq}(S)\xrightarrow{f}\mathscr{F})\in\mathpzc{Fact}_{/\mathscr{F}}}{\mathrm{colim}}\mathrm{Sq}(S)
we have

\mathscr{P}(\mathpzc{Fact}_{/\mathscr{F}}^{\mathrm{op}})\cong\mathscr{P}(\mathpzc{Fact}^{\mathrm{op}})_{/\mathscr{F}}\cong\underset{(\mathrm{Sq}(S)\xrightarrow{f}\mathscr{F})\in\mathpzc{Fact}_{/\mathscr{F}}}{\lim}\mathscr{P}(\mathpzc{Fact}^{\mathrm{op}})_{/\mathrm{Sq}(S)}\cong\underset{(\mathrm{Sq}(S)\xrightarrow{f}\mathscr{F})\in\mathpzc{Fact}_{/\mathscr{F}}}{\lim}\mathscr{P}(\mathpzc{Fact}_{/\mathrm{Sq}(S)}^{\mathrm{op}}).

Moreover, it is easy to see that this isomorphism respects the Segal condition on both sides, so it in fact restricts to the required isomorphism.
∎

Construction 5.5
.

Denote by
\widehat{\mathpzc{Fact}_{/\mathrm{Sq}(S)}}
the following category: its objects are given by strings of morphisms
[m]\overset{i}{\rightarrowtail}[n]\xrightarrow{s}\mathrm{Sq}(S^{\prime})\xrightarrow{f}\mathrm{Sq}(S)
in which
f
is an arbitrary morphism in
\mathrm{Fact}
,
s
is a morphism in
\mathrm{Cat}
such that the image of
[n]
under
s
belongs to
S^{\prime}\subset\mathrm{Sq}(S^{\prime})
,
s(0)
is the initial object of
\mathrm{Sq}(S^{\prime})
and
s(n)
is the final object of
\mathrm{Sq}(S^{\prime})
and
i
is an inert morphism in
\mathpzc{Cat}
. The morphisms are given by commutative diagrams of the form

(13)

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{[m_{0}]}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{[n_{0}]}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{Sq}(S^{\prime}_{0})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{Sq}(S)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{[m_{1}]}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{[n_{1}]}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{Sq}(S^{\prime}_{1})}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces i_{0}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces v}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces w}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces s_{0}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces f}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces u}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces i_{1}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces s_{1}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces g}

in which
u
belongs to
\mathpzc{Fact}^{\mathrm{a.p.}}
.

Proposition 5.6
.

Denote by
\mathrm{Hom}^{\mathrm{lax},!}_{\mathrm{Fact}}(\mathrm{Sq}(S),\mathrm{Span})
the category whose objects are lax functors
F:\mathrm{Sq}(S)\rightsquigarrow\mathrm{Span}
and whose morphisms are given by lax natural transformations
\eta
such that for every object
x\in\mathrm{Sq}(S)
the component
\eta_{x}
is given by a morphism of the form
h_{!}
for a morphism
h
in
\mathscr{S}
. Then there is an equivalence between
\mathrm{Hom}^{\mathrm{lax},!}_{\mathrm{Fact}}(\mathrm{Sq}(S),\mathrm{Span})
and the category of functors
\mathscr{F}:\widehat{\mathpzc{Fact}_{/\mathrm{Sq}(S)}}\rightarrow\mathscr{S}
satisfying the following conditions:

•

for every object
y\overset{\mathrm{def}}{=}([m]\overset{i}{\rightarrowtail}[n]\xrightarrow{s}\mathrm{Sq}(S^{\prime})\xrightarrow{f}\mathrm{Sq}(S))
we have

\mathscr{F}(y)\cong\underset{e\in\widehat{\mathpzc{Fact}_{/\mathrm{Sq}(S)}}^{\mathrm{el}}_{y/}}{\lim}\mathscr{F}(e),

where
\widehat{\mathpzc{Fact}_{/\mathrm{Sq}(S)}}^{\mathrm{el}}_{y/}
is the category whose objects are diagrams of the form

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{[l]}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{[n]}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{Sq}(S^{\prime})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{Sq}(S)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{[m]}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces j}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces i\circ j}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces s}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces f}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces i}

in which
l\in\{0,1\}
and
j
is an inert morphism in
\mathpzc{Cat}
and morphisms are induced by inert morphisms in
\mathpzc{Cat}^{\mathrm{el}}
;

•

\mathscr{F}
takes morphisms of the form

where
j^{\prime}
is an inclusion of the segment
S^{\prime}_{j}
of
S^{\prime}
between
s^{\prime}\circ j(0)
and
s^{\prime}\circ j(n)
, and

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{[m]}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{[n]}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{Sq}(S^{\prime})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{Sq}(S)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{[\widetilde{m}]}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{[n^{\prime}]}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{Sq}(S^{\prime})}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces i}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\widetilde{a}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces a}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces s}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces f}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\widetilde{i}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces s^{\prime}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces f}

to isomorphisms.

Proof.

We begin by providing an isomorphism on the underlying space of objects. First, assume we are given a functor
F:\mathrm{Sq}(S)\rightsquigarrow\mathrm{Span}
. Recall from
3.31
that giving a lax functor
F
is equivalent to giving a functor
\widetilde{\mathscr{L}^{\mathrm{lax}}\mathrm{Sq}(S)}\rightarrow\mathrm{Span}
, where
\widetilde{\mathscr{L}^{\mathrm{lax}}\mathrm{Sq}(S)}
is the category from
3.30
. We can identify
\widetilde{\mathscr{L}^{\mathrm{lax}}\mathrm{Sq}(S)}
with a coCartesian fibration over
\Delta^{\mathrm{op}}
: the value of the corresponding functor on
[n]
is the category whose objects are strings of morphisms
[n]\xrightarrow{s}\mathrm{Sq}(S^{\prime})\xrightarrow{f}\mathrm{Sq}(S)
, where
f
is a morphism in
\mathrm{Fact}
and
s
is a morphism in
\mathrm{Cat}
such that
s([n])\subset S^{\prime}\subset\mathrm{Sq}(S^{\prime})
,
s(0)
is the initial object of
\mathrm{Sq}(S^{\prime})
and
s(n)
is the final object of
\mathrm{Sq}(S^{\prime})
, and whose morphisms are diagrams of the form

}{{{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@transformcm{1.0}{0.0}{0.0}{1.0}{-48.34055pt}{-24.307pt}\lxSVG@begingroup@{transform} \pgfsys@hbox{64}\lxSVG@closescope }}}

{{}{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}}{{}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}{}{{}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces
}{{{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@transformcm{1.0}{0.0}{0.0}{1.0}{33.8758pt}{-22.94594pt}\lxSVG@begingroup@{transform} \pgfsys@hbox{64}\lxSVG@closescope }}}

\lxSVG@closescope
\lxSVG@closescope {\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}\hss}\lxSVG@discardpath\lxSVG@closescope \hss}}\lxSVG@closescope\endpgfpicture}},

where
h
is a morphism in
\mathpzc{Fact}^{\mathrm{a.p.}}
. An inert morphism
j:[n]\rightarrowtail[n^{\prime}]
then induces
j^{*}:\widetilde{\mathscr{L}^{\mathrm{lax}}(\mathrm{Sq}(S))}([n^{\prime}])\rightarrow\widetilde{\mathscr{L}^{\mathrm{lax}}(\mathrm{Sq}(S))}([n])
given by sending
[n^{\prime}]\xrightarrow{s^{\prime}}\mathrm{Sq}(S^{\prime})\xrightarrow{f}\mathrm{Sq}(S)
to
[n]\xrightarrow{s}\mathrm{Sq}(S^{\prime}_{j})\xrightarrow{f}\mathrm{Sq}(S)
and an active morphism
a:[n]\twoheadrightarrow[n^{\prime}]
induces
a^{*}:\widetilde{\mathscr{L}^{\mathrm{lax}}(\mathrm{Sq}(S))}([n^{\prime}])\rightarrow\widetilde{\mathscr{L}^{\mathrm{lax}}(\mathrm{Sq}(S))}([n])
given by sending
[n^{\prime}]\xrightarrow{s^{\prime}}\mathrm{Sq}(S^{\prime})\xrightarrow{f}\mathrm{Sq}(S)
to
[n]\xrightarrow{s^{\prime}\circ a}\mathrm{Sq}(S^{\prime})\xrightarrow{f}\mathrm{Sq}(S)
. Recall from
[
Kos21
, Corollary 2.16.]
that
\mathrm{Span}
, viewed as a coCartesian fibration over
\Delta^{\mathrm{op}}
, has
\mathrm{Span}([n])\cong\mathrm{Cat}^{\mathrm{int}}_{/[m]}
. Here
\mathpzc{Cat}^{\mathrm{int}}_{/[n]}
is the full subcategory of
\mathpzc{Cat}_{/[n]}
on inert morphisms and
\mathrm{Cat}^{\mathrm{int}}_{/[n]}
is the full subcategory of
\mathrm{Hom}_{\mathrm{Cat}}(\mathpzc{Cat}^{\mathrm{int}}_{/[n]},\mathscr{S})
on those functors
G
for which

G([m]\overset{i}{\rightarrowtail}[n])\cong\underset{(j:[m]\rightarrowtail e)\in\mathpzc{Cat}^{\mathrm{el}}_{[m]/}}{\lim}G(e\overset{i\circ j}{\rightarrowtail}[n]).

For an inert morphism
[n]\overset{j}{\rightarrowtail}[n^{\prime}]
we have

j^{*}G([m]\overset{i}{\rightarrowtail}[n])\cong G([m]\overset{j\circ i}{\rightarrowtail}[n^{\prime}])

and for an active morphism
[n]\overset{a}{\twoheadrightarrow}[n^{\prime}]
we have

a^{*}G([m]\overset{i}{\rightarrowtail}[n])\cong G([\widetilde{m}]\overset{\widetilde{i}}{\rightarrowtail}[n^{\prime}]),

where
a\circ i\cong\widetilde{i}\circ\widetilde{a}
is the active/inert factorization in
\mathpzc{Cat}
.

The functor
F
can be identified with a morphism
F:\widetilde{\mathscr{L}^{\mathrm{lax}}\mathrm{Sq}(S)}\rightarrow\mathrm{Span}
of coCartesian fibrations over
\Delta^{\mathrm{op}}
. In particular, for every
n
we have a morphism of categories
F_{n}:\widetilde{\mathscr{L}^{\mathrm{lax}}\mathrm{Sq}(S)}([n])\rightarrow\mathrm{Span}([n])
. Using the definition of
\mathrm{Span}
, we can view
F_{n}
as an element of

\mathrm{Hom}_{\mathrm{Cat}}(\widetilde{\mathscr{L}^{\mathrm{lax}}\mathrm{Sq}(S)}([n]),\mathrm{Hom}_{\mathrm{Cat}}(\mathpzc{Cat}^{\mathrm{int}}_{/[n]},\mathscr{S}))\cong\mathrm{Hom}_{\mathrm{Cat}}(\widetilde{\mathscr{L}^{\mathrm{lax}}\mathrm{Sq}(S)}([n])\times\mathpzc{Cat}^{\mathrm{int}}_{/[n]},\mathscr{S}).

Observe that there is a forgetful functor
U:\widehat{\mathpzc{Fact}_{/\mathrm{Sq}(S)}}\rightarrow\widetilde{\mathscr{L}^{\mathrm{lax}}(\mathrm{Sq}(S))}
that sends
([m]\overset{i}{\rightarrowtail}[n]\xrightarrow{s}\mathrm{Sq}(S^{\prime})\xrightarrow{f}\mathrm{Sq}(S))
to
([n]\xrightarrow{s}\mathrm{Sq}(S^{\prime})\xrightarrow{f}\mathrm{Sq}(S))
. Also denote by
p:\widehat{\mathpzc{Fact}_{/\mathrm{Sq}(S)}}\rightarrow\Delta^{\mathrm{op}}
the morphism that sends
([m]\overset{i}{\rightarrowtail}[n]\xrightarrow{s}\mathrm{Sq}(S^{\prime})\xrightarrow{f}\mathrm{Sq}(S))
to
[n]
. It is easy to see that
p^{-1}([n])\cong\widetilde{\mathscr{L}^{\mathrm{lax}}\mathrm{Sq}(S)}([n])\times\mathpzc{Cat}^{\mathrm{int}}_{/[n]}
, so we can identify the functors
F_{n}
defined above with a functor

\widehat{\mathscr{F}}:\coprod_{n\geq 0}p^{-1}([n])\rightarrow\mathscr{S}.

Also observe that, since all
F_{n}
land in
\mathrm{Span}([n])\subset\mathrm{Hom}_{\mathrm{Cat}}(\mathpzc{Cat}^{\mathrm{int}}_{/[n]},\mathscr{S})
,
\widehat{\mathscr{F}}
satisfies the first condition in our claim. Now we need to extend
\widehat{\mathscr{F}}
to a morphism
\mathscr{F}:\widehat{\mathpzc{Fact}_{/\mathrm{Sq}(S)}}\rightarrow\mathscr{S}
, to do this we need to describe its value on the morphisms that do not lie over identity morphisms in
\Delta^{\mathrm{op}}
. Observe that the morphism in

\lxSVG@closescope {\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}\hss}\lxSVG@discardpath\lxSVG@closescope \hss}}\lxSVG@closescope\endpgfpicture}}.

The bottom morphism lies in
p^{-1}([n_{1}])
, so
\mathscr{F}
is already defined on it too. So it suffices to define
\mathscr{F}
on the top two morphisms. However, observe that, since
F
is a functor, those morphisms are sent to isomorphisms. More precisely, by untangling the identifications we made above we see that

\mathscr{F}([m_{0}]\overset{i_{0}}{\rightarrowtail}[n_{0}]\xrightarrow{u\circ s_{0}}\mathrm{Sq}(S^{\prime}_{0,u})\xrightarrow{g\circ j_{u}}\mathrm{Sq}(S))

can be identified with
F_{n_{0}}(a^{*}([\widetilde{n}_{0}]\xrightarrow{\widetilde{s}_{1}}\mathrm{Sq}(S^{\prime}_{0,u})\xrightarrow{g\circ j_{u}}\mathrm{Sq}(S)))([m_{0}]\overset{i_{0}}{\rightarrowtail}[n_{0}])
, while

\mathscr{F}([\widetilde{m}_{0}]\overset{\widetilde{i}_{0}}{\rightarrowtail}[\widetilde{n}_{0}]\xrightarrow{\widetilde{s}_{1}}\mathrm{Sq}(S^{\prime}_{0,u})\xrightarrow{g\circ j_{u}}\mathrm{Sq}(S))

can be identified with
a^{*}(F_{\widetilde{n}_{0}}([\widetilde{n}_{0}]\xrightarrow{\widetilde{s}_{1}}\mathrm{Sq}(S^{\prime}_{0,u})\xrightarrow{g\circ j_{u}}\mathrm{Sq}(S)))([m_{0}]\overset{i_{0}}{\rightarrowtail}[n_{0}])
. Since
F
is assumed to be a functor, we have
a^{*}\circ F_{\widetilde{n}_{0}}\cong F_{n_{0}}\circ a^{*}
, which proves that these two spaces are isomorphic. Similarly, the value

\mathscr{F}([\widetilde{m}_{0}]\overset{\widetilde{i}_{0}}{\rightarrowtail}[\widetilde{n}_{0}]\xrightarrow{\widetilde{s}_{1}}\mathrm{Sq}(S^{\prime}_{0,u})\xrightarrow{g\circ j_{u}}\mathrm{Sq}(S))

can be identified with
F_{\widetilde{n}_{0}}(i^{*}([n_{1}]\xrightarrow{s_{1}}\mathrm{Sq}(S^{\prime}_{1})\xrightarrow{g}\mathrm{Sq}(S)))([\widetilde{m}_{0}]\overset{\widetilde{i}_{0}}{\rightarrowtail}[\widetilde{n}_{0}])
, while

\mathscr{F}([\widetilde{m}_{1}]\overset{i\circ\widetilde{i}_{0}}{\rightarrowtail}[n_{1}]\xrightarrow{s_{1}}\mathrm{Sq}(S^{\prime}_{1})\xrightarrow{g}\mathrm{Sq}(S))

can be identified with
i^{*}(F_{n_{1}}([n_{1}]\xrightarrow{s_{1}}\mathrm{Sq}(S^{\prime}_{1})\xrightarrow{g}\mathrm{Sq}(S)))([\widetilde{m}_{0}]\overset{\widetilde{i}_{0}}{\rightarrowtail}[\widetilde{n}_{0}])
, those values are once again isomorphic by functoriality of
F
. We have thus defined the required morphism
\mathscr{F}:\widehat{\mathpzc{Fact}_{/\mathrm{Sq}(S)}}\rightarrow\mathscr{S}
, moreover, the morphisms
(\widetilde{a},a)
and
(i,j_{u})
appearing in the diagram (
14
) are precisely the morphisms mentioned in the second condition of
5.6
, so
\mathscr{F}
indeed satisfies it since it sends them to isomorphisms by construction. Conversely, given a functor
\mathscr{F}
as above that satisfies the conditions of the proposition, we can define
F:\widetilde{\mathscr{L}^{\mathrm{lax}}(\mathrm{Sq}(S))}\rightarrow\mathrm{Span}
by setting
F_{n}
to be equal to the restriction
\mathscr{F}|_{p^{-1}([n])}
. The fact that
F_{n}
lands in
\mathrm{Span}([n])\subset\mathrm{Hom}_{\mathrm{Cat}}(\mathpzc{Cat}^{\mathrm{int}}_{/[n]},\mathscr{S})
then follows from the first condition of the proposition and the fact that
F
is a functor from the second. This establishes the required isomorphism on the spaces of objects.

To prove the isomorphism on morphisms, observe that a lax natural transformation
\alpha:F\rightarrow G
amounts to giving a morphism
\alpha_{X}:F(x)\rightarrow G(x)
for every object
x\in\mathrm{Sq}(S)
together with, for each morphism
v:x\rightarrow y
represented by
([i]\rightarrowtail[1]\xrightarrow{s}\mathrm{Sq}(S^{\prime})\xrightarrow{f}\mathrm{Sq}(S))
, morphisms

\alpha_{v}\in\mathrm{Hom}_{\mathrm{Span}(F(x),G(y))}(\alpha_{y,!}\circ F(v),G(v)\circ\alpha_{x,!})\cong\mathrm{Hom}_{\mathscr{S}_{/F(x)\times F(y)}}(F(v),\alpha^{*}_{y}\circ G(v)\circ\alpha_{x,!}),

where the isomorphism follows since
\alpha_{y,!}
and
\alpha^{*}_{y}
are adjoint and
\mathrm{Span}(F(x),F(y))\cong\mathscr{S}_{/F(x)\times F(y)}
, such that
\alpha_{v}
are compatible with units and compositions. In other words, if we identify
F(v)
and
G(v)
with spans
(F(x)\leftarrow F(v)\rightarrow F(y))
and
(G(x)\leftarrow G(v)\rightarrow G(y))
, then
\alpha_{v}
is a morphism
F(v)\rightarrow G(v)
making the following diagram commute

This can be identified with a morphism from
F(v)
to
G(v)
in
\mathpzc{Cat}^{\mathrm{int}}_{/[1]}
. The data of
\alpha_{u}
for all morphisms
u
can then be identified with a morphism
\widetilde{\alpha}_{1}:\mathscr{F}|_{p^{-1}([1])}\rightarrow\mathscr{G}|_{p^{-1}([1])}
, where
\mathscr{F}
and
\mathscr{G}
are the objects of
\mathrm{Hom}_{\mathrm{Cat}}(\widehat{\mathpzc{Fact}_{/\mathrm{Sq}(S)}},\mathscr{S})
corresponding to
F
and
G
, and the fact that
\alpha
is a natural transformation then means that
\widetilde{\alpha}_{1}
can be extended to
\widetilde{\alpha}:\mathscr{F}\rightarrow\mathscr{G}
. Conversely, for every
\widetilde{\alpha}:\mathscr{F}\rightarrow\mathscr{G}
we can construct the corresponding lax natural transformation by setting
\alpha_{x}
to be the restriction of
\widetilde{\alpha}
to
([0]=\joinrel=[0]\rightarrow*\xrightarrow{x}\mathrm{Sq}(S))
and
\alpha_{v}
to be the restriction of
\widetilde{\alpha}
to
([1]=\joinrel=[1]\rightarrow\mathrm{Sq}(S^{\prime})\xrightarrow{v}\mathrm{Sq}(S))
. This establishes the required equivalence.
∎

Theorem 5.7
.

For any
\mathscr{F}\in\mathrm{Fact}
we have an equivalence of categories

\mathrm{Hom}_{\mathrm{Fact}}^{\mathrm{lax},!}(\mathscr{F},\mathrm{Span})\cong\mathrm{Fact}_{/\mathscr{F}}.

Proof.

By
5.4
and
3.22
it suffices to prove the claim for
\mathscr{F}\cong\mathrm{Sq}(S)
for some
S\in\mathpzc{Fact}
. In this case, we can further use
5.6
and
5.3
to reduce our claim to the following simpler statement: there is an equivalence between the category of presheaves on
(\mathpzc{Fact}_{/\mathrm{Sq}(S)})^{\mathrm{op}}
satisfying the Segal condition and the category of presheaves on
\widehat{\mathpzc{Fact}_{/\mathrm{Sq}(S)}}
satisfying the conditions of
5.6
. To prove this, first denote by
\overline{p}:\widehat{\mathpzc{Fact}_{/\mathrm{Sq}(S)}}\rightarrow\mathpzc{Fact}_{/\mathrm{Sq}(S)}
the morphism that sends
([m]\overset{i}{\rightarrowtail}[n]\xrightarrow{s}\mathrm{Sq}(S^{\prime})\xrightarrow{f}\mathrm{Sq}(S))
to
(\mathrm{Sq}(S^{\prime}_{i})\xrightarrow{f}\mathrm{Sq}(S))
, where
S^{\prime}_{i}
is the substring of
S^{\prime}
on those segments that lie between
s\circ i(0)
and
s\circ i(m)
and sends a morphism represented by diagram (
13
) to the induce morphism
u_{i}:\mathrm{Sq}(S^{\prime}_{i_{0}})\rightarrow\mathrm{Sq}(S^{\prime}_{i_{1}})
over
\mathrm{Sq}(S)
. Denote by
K\subset\widehat{\mathpzc{Fact}_{/\mathrm{Sq}(S)}}
the subcategory generated by morphisms described in the second condition of
5.6
and denote by
\widehat{\mathpzc{Fact}_{/\mathrm{Sq}(S)}}[K^{-1}]
the category obtained by inverting those morphisms. Observe that every functor
\mathscr{F}:\widehat{\mathpzc{Fact}_{/\mathrm{Sq}(S)}}\rightarrow\mathscr{S}
that satisfies the conditions of
5.6
(particularly the second condition) factors uniquely through
\widehat{\mathpzc{Fact}_{/\mathrm{Sq}(S)}}[K^{-1}]
. Also observe that the morphism
\overline{p}
sends all the morphisms in
K
to identities, so it also factors as

We will prove that the functor
p
in the diagram above is in fact an equivalence of categories. This will imply that the category of presheaves on
(\mathpzc{Fact}_{/\mathrm{Sq}(S)})^{\mathrm{op}}
is equivalent to the category of presheaves on
\widehat{\mathpzc{Fact}_{/\mathrm{Sq}(S)}}^{\mathrm{op}}
satisfying the second condition of
5.6
. Moreover, it will be clear from the construction of the equivalence that the presheaves on
(\mathpzc{Fact}_{/\mathrm{Sq}(S)})^{\mathrm{op}}
that satisfy the Segal condition would correspond to those presheaves on
\widehat{\mathpzc{Fact}_{/\mathrm{Sq}(S)}}^{\mathrm{op}}
that additionally satisfy the first condition of
5.6
, thus concluding the proof of our claim.

In order to prove that those categories are equivalent we first construct a morphism
s:\mathpzc{Fact}_{/\mathrm{Sq}(S)}\rightarrow\widehat{\mathpzc{Fact}_{/\mathrm{Sq}(S)}}[K^{-1}]
. On objects
s
acts by sending
\mathrm{Sq}(S^{\prime})\xrightarrow{f}\mathrm{Sq}(S)
to
([1]=\joinrel=[1]\xrightarrow{a}\mathrm{Sq}(S^{\prime})\xrightarrow{f}\mathrm{Sq}(S))
, where
a
is the morphism that sends
0
to the initial and
1
the final object of
\mathrm{Sq}(S^{\prime})
. By
2.6
every morphism in
\mathpzc{Fact}_{/\mathrm{Sq}(S)}
decomposes into elementary morphisms, so in order to describe the action of
s
on morphisms it suffices to describe it on the elementary morphisms. Assume
e:\mathrm{Sq}(S^{\prime}_{0})\rightarrow\mathrm{Sq}(S^{\prime}_{1})
is an elementary morphism over
\mathrm{Sq}(S)
that belongs to
\mathpzc{Fact}^{\mathrm{a.p.}}
, in that case we define
s(e)
to be given by the diagram

Now assume that the elementary morphism has the form
\delta^{h}_{0}
(the same argument will work for any other elementary inclusion). This morphism is sent to

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{[1]}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{[1]}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{Sq}(S^{\prime}_{0})}

(observe that the bottom morphism in this diagram is invertible in

to

we take the inverse of it), where

into

is sent to the first segment of

p\circ s\cong\mathrm{id}
. In order to finish the proof we will construct an equivalence
\eta:s\circ p\xrightarrow{\sim}\mathrm{id}

for every object

corresponds to the string

Observe that both of the morphisms appearing in the definition of
\eta_{x}
lie in
K
, so is is indeed an isomorphism. To prove that
\eta
defines a natural transformation we need to show that for any morphism
g:x\rightarrow y
in
\widehat{\mathpzc{Fact}_{/\mathrm{Sq}(S)}}
the following naturality square

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces x}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces y}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces s\circ p(x)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces s\circ p(y)}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces g}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\eta_{x}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces s\circ p(g)}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\eta_{y}}

commutes. Assume that the morphism
g
is represented by the diagram (
13
). We begin by decomposing it as

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{[m_{0}]}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{[n_{0}]}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{Sq}(S_{0}^{\prime})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{[\widetilde{m}_{0}]}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{[\widetilde{n}_{0}]}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{Sq}(S^{\prime}_{0,u})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{Sq}(S)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{[m_{1}]}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{[n_{1}]}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{Sq}(S^{\prime}_{1})}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces i_{0}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\widetilde{a}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces a}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces s_{0}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces u^{\prime}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces f}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\widetilde{i}_{0}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces i_{2}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\widetilde{s}_{1}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces i}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces j_{u}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces g\circ j_{u}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces i_{1}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces s_{1}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces g}

using the notation from the proof of
5.6
. It suffices to prove the commutativity of the naturality squares separately for the top and bottom morphism, we start from the top. Observe that the image under
p
of the top morphism belongs to
\mathpzc{Fact}^{\mathrm{a.p.}}
. The relevant commutative diagram is a large three-dimensional diagram, for convenience we will replace it with three commutative squares given by the images of that diagram under
p_{1}:\widehat{\mathpzc{Fact}_{/\mathrm{Sq}(S)}}\rightarrow\mathpzc{Cat}
,
p_{2}:\widehat{\mathpzc{Fact}_{/\mathrm{Sq}(S)}}\rightarrow\mathpzc{Cat}
and
p_{3}:\widehat{\mathpzc{Fact}_{/\mathrm{Sq}(S)}}\rightarrow\mathpzc{Fact}
, where
p_{1}
sends
([m]\overset{i}{\rightarrowtail}[n]\xrightarrow{s}\mathrm{Sq}(S^{\prime})\xrightarrow{f}\mathrm{Sq}(S))
to
[m]
,
p_{2}
to
[n]
and
p_{3}
to
\mathrm{Sq}(S^{\prime})
. Using this convention and the definition of
s
on morphisms in
\mathpzc{Fact}^{\mathrm{a.p.}}
, we see that the diagrams have the form

The fact that these diagrams commute is obvious. Now we need to prove the commutativity of the naturality squares for the bottom morphism. Observe that we can further decompose
j_{u}
into elementary inclusions, so we can assume that
j_{u}\cong\delta^{h}_{0}
. In this case
i\cong\delta_{0}
and
i_{2}
is either
\mathrm{id}
or
\delta_{0}
. The case of
i_{2}\cong\mathrm{id}
is trivial, so we can assume
i_{2}\cong\delta_{0}
. In this case the relevant diagrams are

\lxSVG@closescope {\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}\hss}\lxSVG@discardpath\lxSVG@closescope \hss}}\lxSVG@closescope\endpgfpicture}},

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{[1]}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{[2]}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{[1]}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{[\widetilde{m}_{0}]}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{[\widetilde{m}_{0}+1]}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{[\widetilde{n}_{0}]}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{[\widetilde{n}_{0}+1]}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\delta_{0}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\delta_{1}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\delta_{0}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\widetilde{i}_{0}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces i_{1}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\delta_{0}}

and

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{Sq}(S^{\prime}_{0,u,\widetilde{i}_{0}})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{Sq}(S^{\prime}_{1,i_{1}})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{Sq}(S^{\prime}_{0,u})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{Sq}(S^{\prime}_{1})}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces j^{\prime}_{\widetilde{i}_{0}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\delta^{h}_{0}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces j^{\prime}_{i_{1}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces j_{u}}

(we again implicitly invert the morphisms that are ”pointing the wrong way”). The commutativity of the third diagram is obvious. To prove the commutativity of the outer rectangles in the first two diagrams it would suffice to provide the dotted arrow shown on the diagrams that would make the inner rectangle and the triangle on the right commute. This arrow should be given by a morphism from

([2]=\joinrel=[2]\xrightarrow{a^{\prime}}\mathrm{Sq}(S^{\prime}_{1,i_{1}})\xrightarrow{g^{\prime}}\mathrm{Sq}(S))

to

([\widetilde{m}_{0}+1]\overset{i_{1}}{\rightarrowtail}[\widetilde{n}_{0}+1]\xrightarrow{s_{1}}\mathrm{Sq}(S^{\prime}_{1})\xrightarrow{g}\mathrm{Sq}(S)),

where
a^{\prime}
is a morphism sending
1<2
to
S^{\prime}_{0,u,\widetilde{i}_{0}}
and
0<1
to the first segment in
S^{\prime}_{1,i_{1}}
. We define this morphism to be given by the diagram

where
a^{\prime\prime}
denotes a morphism sending
0
to
0
,
1
to
1
and
2
to
(\widetilde{m}_{0}+1)
. That this morphism makes the relevant diagrams commute is elementary, and with that we have finished the proof of the claim.
∎

Corollary 5.8
.

The category
\mathrm{Fact}
is isomorphic to the category of distributive laws in
\mathrm{Span}
.

Proof.

It follows by applying
theorem

5.7
to the case
\mathscr{F}\cong*
.
∎
